Miami's Scarlett Schremmer DQ'd from LPGA event for unauthorized yardage book

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Scarlett Schremmer's LPGA debut will now end in disqualification, not with a missed cut.

The Miami sophomore was determined by LPGA officials to have used an unauthorized yardage book during competition at The Annika driven by Gainbridge, where Schremmer was competing on a sponsor exemption and shot 69-74.

The LPGA, which first learned about the potential infraction on Thursday before reviewing photo evidence following Friday's second round, issued the following statement: "Schremmer violated Model Local Rule G-11, which, per the LPGA Rules of Play Card, is in effect during all LPGA Tour competitions, except at off-site local qualifying events. This Model Local Rule requires that players are limited throughout their round to using only the yardage book that has been approved for use in the competition."

Schremmer later released her own statement, confirming that her yardage book had illegal green-reading materials in it.

"I had a green reading yardage book in my bag this week that's allowed in college golf but isn't permitted on the LPGA Tour," Schremmer wrote. "That's on me. I should have checked, and it's a lesson learned. I had a great week competing out here and I'm grateful to the Doyle family, Pelican and the LPGA for the opportunity. I am excited to continue to work with the goal of playing out here again. Thank you to everyone who came out and supported me this week."

Prior to Schremmer's DQ, one of her playing competitors, Perrine Delacour, took to social media on Saturday morning, commenting on a Monday Q Info post, "Can we talk about the yardage [book] illegal and LPGA trust the sponsor invite over 2 LPGA players and 2 caddies."

Delacour declined to elaborate when reached by Golfweek's Beth Ann Nichols, instead saying that she handled the matter with the LPGA.

Schremmer recently wrapped up her fall semester with the Hurricanes, during which she won twice, and she enters winter break ranked 17th in the country.

Ole Miss had one job: Get to Oklahoma. But SEC bailed it out because of CFP pipe dream

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

It's less than eight hours from Oxford, Mississippi to Norman, Oklahoma. A police escort could shave an hour off the run.

So why didn't Ole Miss simply drive to play Oklahoma after the FAA ground stop Friday afternoon?

Because for some unknown reason, Ole Miss more than likely believes it still has an outside chance to reach the College Football Playoff, that's why.

CFP rankings live predictions: How will Week 11 games shake up bracket?

More: Oklahoma AD calls out SEC, 'carnival barker' Finebaum after Ole Miss game rescheduled

They won't say it, and their official statement doesn't address it, but that's what this is about.

Ole Miss didn't make the drive and will play Oklahoma on Sunday afternoon, because Ole Miss didn't want to play the game after driving through the early hours of Saturday morning. Didn't want to put any undue stress on a team that needs to be absolutely perfect over the final three weeks of the season — and needs plenty of help outside its orbit to reach the CFP.

But Ole Miss can't say that, can't admit to that last-gasp hope of returning the CFP, and have the SEC agree to a Sunday game. So they cooked up a story about flight personnel and their expiring clocks, and it sure sounds good because it's more than likely true.

But Ole Miss was informed of the ground stop at the same time as every other college football team preparing to play a road game this weekend. Every other team made it work.

So Ole Miss and Oklahoma will play Sunday because the SEC, for some reason, told Oklahoma that Ole Miss tried, and it will be in Norman for the game Sunday. And if the Sooners aren't, they forfeit the game.

Check me if I'm wrong here, Sparky, but that's exactly what the SEC should've said to Ole Miss. The game is Saturday at 9 or 10 p.m. local, be there or forfeit the game.

Short of an act of God, we play football on Saturdays. If that means you're inconvenienced, so be it.

We're all professionals, we all get paid to play and coach a game.

And I don't want to hear self-serving statements from Oklahoma or Ole Miss or the SEC about "the health and welfare" of our student-athletes. That ridiculous 24-team CFP you just signed off on puts eight teams in line to potentially play 17 games, beginning in 2027.

They're 18-22-year-olds (unless we're talking about Indiana, circa 2025), they're young men in the best shape of their lives. They can deal with a seven-hour bus trip, and getting into Norman in the early morning hours of Saturday.

When the FAA announced the nation-wide ground stop at 3:54 p.m. ET (2:54 CT) Friday afternoon, Ole Miss should've immediately made alternate plans to travel by bus — while trying find other flight accommodations.

Give yourself a deadline: If we don't have information about definite flight plans by 8 p.m., we're driving to Oklahoma. We'll get in town by 4 a.m., get our eight hours of sleep, and begin preparing for a 9 or 10 p.m. CT kick.

If Ole Miss had informed the SEC they were driving, the SEC would've moved the game to whatever time worked for both teams. Oklahoma would've played at any time.

Instead, Ole Miss waited until the last possible minute to declare we can't get a plane, we can't get a flight crew, we can't get whatever we need to make it work. So we're not playing Saturday.

And that's just not how it works. At 4 p.m. ET, the SEC should've told all member schools traveling to football games that there's no excuse for not playing the game on Saturday.

Find a way to get to the game, even if you have to drive. Or forfeit the game.

Matt Hayes is the senior national college football writer for USA TODAY Sports Network. Follow him on X at @MattHayesCFB. Listen to him daily, from 12-2 p.m., on 1010XL-Jacksonville.

10 Hilarious Blondie Comics That Mock The Modern Day

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Since 1930, Blondie and Dagwood Bumstead have been navigating domestic life, delighting readers with jokes and comedic moments that have stood the test of time. Blondie’s success is no small feat: it is one of the longest-running and most influential comic strips of all time, even making a cameo in other popular strips like Garfield.

Blondie achieved remarkable longevity because it proved fertile ground for satirical strips that mock modern life. These strips contrast traditional mid-century sensibilities with today’s hyperconnected, high-speed reality. These iconic characters are placed in situations that hold a mirror up to readers and mock what has become commonplace in modern life.

This article highlights 10 Blondie comics that mock the modern day, showcasing the long-running series at its sharpest and funniest. By juxtaposing classic strip tropes with the chaotic landscape of smartphones, selfies and modern trends, Blondie has perfected the formula for comedy gold.

This comic strip satirises how obsessed people have become with their smartphones. Something as simple as reading a book has become a difficult task in an era when people’s attention spans have been greatly reduced by social media and fast-paced, easily accessible content.

The strip opens with Dagwood asking his daughter what book she is reading, and she replies, “A book for my book report that’s due tomorrow.” Dagwood, still thinking that’s a good thing, replies, “Well, better late than never, right?” but she comically reveals that she hasn’t started the book at all, showing her smartphone hidden among the books.

This joke pokes fun at how smartphones have made procrastination so easy. It is now easier than ever to get distracted or spend hours scrolling, even when deadlines need to be met. Many people would rather scroll for hours than spend an hour reading a book and doing their homework.

Technology has made many things easier, both at home and in the workplace, yet it still cannot load your dishes for you. This strip satirises how the rise of new technology has made people lazier than ever. Even something as simple as loading dishes into a dishwasher is something people wish an app could handle.

In the strip, Dagwood comments that “there’s literally an App for Everything”, and Blondie agrees and immediately chips in, saying, “ Too bad there isn’t an app for loading the Dishes”, and the next panel is Dagwood loading up the dishes and lamenting that “there should be an app for this”.

Beyond being a hilarious strip, it mocks how dependent people have become on technology, even for everyday tasks. It also offers an ironic commentary on how men avoid household chores, even though they expect their wives to do them.

Social media platforms such as Facebook are a good way to stay in touch with friends and family; however, the access they provide can often allow others to criticise or comment on things that involve other people’s personal lives. Additionally, it can lead to unintended consequences, like flirting, in an attempt to defend someone online.

The strip opens with Blondie commenting on how much time Dagwood has been spending on the “New neighbourhood Facebook page”, and Dagwood chips in about how much people have been complaining about Barbie Babewlder sunning herself in her bikini. Blondie makes a snarky comment about how he couldn’t help defending her, and Dagwood claims he can’t help being a peacemaker.

Unfortunately, the tables turn when Blondie sits down at the computer and says, “Speaking of sunning, Hank Hunkerman is cleaning his pool”. It catches Dagwood by surprise, and he then understands how Blondie felt earlier. He had unintentionally been crossing boundaries and flirting, but social media normalises it and makes it possible.

It is commonplace these days to make fun of young people for being addicted to social media, and not without merit. Using Alexander, Dagwood’s teenage son, this strip highlights how people are less likely to go outside for a walk because they are too busy scrolling on social media.

In the strip, Alexander complains that “reels” on Instagram and Facebook are driving him crazy. He then asks his Dad how he can escape all the reels on Social media, and Dagwood replies, “Have you tried ‘real life’?. Nobody is forced to scroll on social media against their will; some people just need a reminder to log off.

The world today is full of so many issues that some may find it insensitive to have silly, goofy, lighthearted days at work. Still, employers continue to find ways to break up the monotony of a 9-to-5 with fun workplace activities. The current sociopolitical climate may be concerning, but having some fun at work won’t make things any worse.

In this strip, Dagwood’s office decides to have a funny T-shirt day to commemorate the first day of summer, much to the dismay of one of his coworkers, who comments, “Really? In today’s world?” The office is filled with people wearing fun T-shirts, like the ironic “I love money” T-shirt Dagwood’s boss wears and a coworker’s Garfield T-shirt.

It’s pretty much a fact that Dagwood is lazy and would rather do anything else than chores. Nonetheless, this strip makes fun of how people often put on television to gain motivation to clean and do chores. It’s very common today to throw on something to watch in the background because people need that extra stimulation.

In this strip, Blondie tells Dagwood they have a lot of spring cleaning to do, since she has prepared an official cleaning list. Dagwood, trying to avoid chores, offers “the perfect way to get motivated”, which turns out to be a suggestion to watch an hour of HGTV and westerns, which Blondie soundly rejects.

In the modern age, information is easy to access, but misinformation is just as widespread. The internet makes it easy for misinformation to spread through unregulated forums and social media platforms that let people post whatever they like. Blondie satirises the spread of celebrity rumours by using iconic Simpsons characters.

The strip features Dagwood in bed with Blondie, using an iPad, and he complains about fake online rumours about a celebrity couple. Asked who, he says Homer and Marge Simpson, and Blondie remarks that it must be a slow news day. Readers can relate to clickbait titles and articles engineered to farm engagement.

In this strip, Blondie perfectly captures how corny it is when middle-aged people use Facebook and chat-speak. Today, it’s not uncommon for people to spend hours chatting with friends and family, even at work, often neglecting important and pressing duties.

In the strip, Dagwood is on his computer and sees that his friend Woodley has been on Facebook for an hour. Unaware of the irony that he is also goofing off on Facebook, he tells Woodley to get off Facebook and do his job. The two, along with Dwitsel Tweezer, then spend the next few panels goofing off and trash-talking.

This is interrupted when Dagwood’s boss walks into the room and asks whether he has finished the Heinwoofer Contract, and he replies, “Not yet, Boss! But I’ve been typing like a madman all morning!” In all fairness, he wasn’t technically lying; still, goofing off at work like this has become a common occurrence these days.

Blogging about your personal life online or posting life updates has become normal in today’s society, but Blondie shows readers how bizarre it really is. People detail their entire lives online, yet still expect a certain level of privacy, which can seem contradictory.

It begins with a panel showing Dagwood’s daughter sitting at the computer, and Dagwood walks up to ask what she is doing. She says she is blogging about her personal life online, and in the next panel she covers the screen with her hands and asks her father not to look, saying it is private.

Ordinarily, if you’re too ashamed to show your father something, you probably shouldn’t post it online, but these days it may be stranger not to. Social media keeps blurring the line between what should be public and what should be private, making the strip a hilarious piece of satire and irony.

The telephone has long evolved beyond what its inventor, Alexander Graham Bell, envisioned. Comparing the first phone with a modern smartphone makes the smartphone seem like pure wizardry. Blondie uses this premise to create a strip that mocks how people mainly use these devices to send cute cat videos and text jokes to their friends.

The first panel of the strip shows Dagwood conversing with a co-worker by a water dispenser, when the co-worker asks, “What would Alexander Graham Bell do if he saw how his invention has turned into this crazy smartphone Mania?” Dagwood sharply replied that he would send his friend Watson a cute cat video.

His co-worker ironically decided to text the joke to his buddy Ziggy. Not only does the strip explore how far phones have evolved, but it also comments on people’s obsession with and reliance on smartphones. The co-worker couldn’t help but text his buddy a joke he had just heard.

Marvel Officially Announces New 'Angela' Series

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Marvel has announced a new series starring Angela, the forgotten daughter of Odin and sister of Thor and Loki. Originally a character in the Spawn universe, Marvel acquired the rights to the character in 2013, introducing her as an Asgardian raised in the forgotten realm of 'Heven.'

The daughter of Odin and Freyja, Angela (born 'Aldrif') was kidnapped by Angels and spirited away to the Tenth Realm, which was locked off from the Nine Realms of classic Thor lore. Thor and Loki later went on a mission to free her, with the character starring in her own solo comics as well as appearing in Guardians of the Galaxy and Strikeforce.

Angela was partially adapted in the MCU's Thor: Ragnarok, with elements of her backstory given to classic villain Hela. Now, Angela is getting her own series as Marvel takes the character back to basics.

Marvel's new ongoing Angela series will see the character descend on New York as a demon hunter, working on a bounty from Heven itself. Coming February 2026 from Wes Craig and Pere Pérez, the new series will see Angela face off against the demon lord Abraxis, seemingly sidelining past lore to give fans a perfect jumping-on point for the character.

Angela was last seen as a supporting character in Doctor Strange, helping the sorcerer during his time in Asgard. Now, she has a brand new mission as Marvel reveals:

Demons are running amok in New York City, and a new demon hunter has been assigned by Heven to hunt and destroy them - Angela. Are things not what they seem? What does the demon lord Abraxis want with the Big Apple? Why is Heven so concerned with Earth? Angela doesn’t really care but plans to wet her blade with evil blood.

Marvel has also shared covers by Kaare Andrews and Pérez, showcasing Angela's golden armor and razor-like ribbons. Her enemy in this series is the demonic Abraxis, who shouldn't be confused for Abraxas - a multiversal predator held at bay by the existence of Galactus.

It's possible that Angela will spin out of the newly launched Avengers Volume 10, in which Mephisto's scheming will seemingly unleash demons on the city.

Despite multiple solo series and a dedicated fanbase, Angela has never truly been integrated into Marvel's Earth-616, rarely interacting with heroes who aren't directly linked to Asgard. The new Angela series seems set to change that, basing her in Marvel's hero capital of New York and giving her a unique role as a dedicated demon hunter.

Part of Marvel's decision in publishing a new Angela series seems to be based on her appearance in Marvel Rivals. The hero shooter game has increased Angela's profile, as she appears alongside icons like Captain America and Hulk, familiarizing casual fans with her attitude and abilities.

Speaking to Marvel, editor Nick Lowe appears to confirm that the series is intended to cater to fans who know Angela as a character, but haven't followed her stories thus far, saying:

Is Angela Thor’s sister? Yes. Was she a Guardian of the Galaxy? Yes. Does she care about either of those? Hel no! Angela’s focused on her new assignment from Heven - rid New York City of the epidemic of demons currently plaguing it and you get to read all about it in her new ongoing series!

Angela is relatively unique in being a character who began outside Marvel but was then given a very personal connection to it, introduced into sprawling Asgardian lore. Writer Wes Craig emphasizes Angela's nature as a loner character and the challenges that poses, saying:

Writing my first Marvel series has been very cool. It's such a fun world to play in. Angela's an old school warrior. Stoic, no-nonsense, she doesn't suffer fools, doesn't have time for BS, and she's tough as nails. I love that kind of character. But that also makes her an interesting challenge: What would make someone like that show us her heart? And is there anything that would make her break? Over the next year, we're gonna find out...

Artist Pere Pérez reveals a personal connection to Angela going back to her Spawn era, confirming there'll be plenty of classic fantasy combat:

I'm having a crazy amount of fun drawing this series. Wes and I are totally on the same page with the creative choices for Angela, and he is writing a very fun and layered story. One of the very first comics I bought with my own money was the original Angela miniseries from the ‘90s, so being able to add my own twists to her design has been very exciting. I love drawing monsters and demons, and I've been wanting to draw fight scenes involving weapons like... swords, axes, spears and medieval stuff, so expect some crazy action! This book is going to make you feel as if a heavy metal song is blasting in your brain!

Over the last decade, Angela has had some great stories in Marvel Comics, but has never felt like a true Marvel character. That's seemingly about to change as Angela begins a new mission in New York, hunting an army of demons and unavoidably crossing paths with some of Marvel's biggest heroes.

8 Neo-Western Shows That Define The Genre

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The western was one of the first TV genres to gain traction. Like cop shows and variety shows and classic situation comedies, the western is an enduring staple of the television landscape, and like those other genres, it never goes away; it just evolves. Back in the TV western’s heyday, old-school cowboy shows like Lancer and Wagon Train and The Virginian dominated the airwaves. But audiences eventually fell out of love with their tired tropes and rigid formulas and romanticized view of the ugliest period in American history, and the genre had to adapt with the times.

Now, the airwaves (or, rather, streaming-waves) are dominated by neo-westerns, subverting the conventions of those old classics. Neo-westerns can either bring the tropes of westerns into a modern-day setting, like Yellowstone and Justified, or they can do something else entirely, like the spacefaring adventures of Cowboy Bebop or the philosophical meditations of HBO’s Westworld.

Taylor Sheridan has singlehandedly revitalized the TV western with the Yellowstone saga. Yellowstone has proven to be so popular that it’s no longer just a hit show in its own right; it’s been expanded into a Marvel-sized cinematic universe of sequels, prequels, and spinoffs. We’ve seen different eras of the Dutton family, different corners of the Yellowstone universe, and different chapters of the overall timeline.

But the flagship show remains the most compelling. Yellowstone brings the land disputes of Shane and the complicated family dynamics of East of Eden and the horse-wrangling antics of The Cowboys into a modern setting. The show relaunched Kevin Costner’s career and made Sheridan the Shonda Rhimes of dad TV.

Walker, Texas Ranger ran for the better part of a decade, and its success had nothing to do with the subtlety of its storytelling or the profundity of its themes or the poignancy of its character development; it had everything to do with the irresistible star power of Chuck Norris. Norris was one of the greatest action heroes to take to the screen, and Walker, Texas Ranger was the perfect vehicle for his talents.

Viewers spent nine seasons tuning in to watch Norris’ U.S. Marine-turned-Texas Ranger/kung fu teacher Cordell Walker use his martial arts skills to bring bad guys to justice. Walker, Texas Ranger was the last classic TV western before Deadwood and Hell on Wheels would bring a gritty realism to the genre, and it ran for more than 200 episodes.

Kurt Sutter’s creation of Sons of Anarchy was primarily influenced by Hamlet. It reimagines all the archetypes from William Shakespeare’s seminal play — the grieving son, the usurping stepdad, the morally gray mother — and filtered them through the lens of a contemporary California biker gang. Sons of Anarchy is essentially The Sopranos on wheels. It has all the crime family dynamics of The Sopranos, contrasting relatable family situations with life-or-death stakes, but with the rubber-burning thrills of hitting the open road atop a Harley.

While you could accurately describe it as Hamlet meets The Sopranos on wheels, Sons of Anarchy’s setting is very western-coded. Jax Teller and his fellow bikers are modern-day cowboys — on a steel horse, they ride — and all the chases along dusty roads and armed standoffs in the desert feel very analogous to classic TV westerns.

Michael Crichton’s original Westworld film was a pretty straightforward chase movie. The theme park full of robotic cowboys was just a novel setting for a relentless cat-and-mouse pursuit between Yul Brynner’s unstoppable gunslinger and Richard Benjamin’s frightened tourist. But Jonathan Nolan and Lisa Joy took the bones of that old B-movie and turned it into a complex, thought-provoking philosophical examination of artificial intelligence for HBO.

Westworld was poised to be HBO’s sci-fi alternative to Game of Thrones. Where Thrones had branched outside fantasy fans to attract mainstream viewers, Westworld hoped to do the same. Ultimately, it was much too complicated to have that kind of impact. But even just as a western fan, it was fun to see such a grand, cinematic show deconstruct the genre in such an experimental and esoteric way on such a huge, ambitious scale.

Adapted from Tony Hillerman’s Leaphorn & Chee books, Dark Winds essentially brings a neo-western edge to an old-school cop show. It combines elements of westerns, police noirs, and psychological thrillers in a chilling tale of three Navajo Tribal Police officers patrolling the Four Corners area in the American Southwest, circa the ‘70s.

It mixes the frontier justice of a western like Gunslinger with the mystery and intrigue of a neo-noir like Chinatown. Zahn McClarnon shines in the lead role of grizzled cop Joe Leaphorn, and his uneasy alliance with a border patrol agent and an undercover federal officer provides a compelling dramatic anchor for the series.

Cowboy Bebop was the gateway anime that opened a lot of western viewers’ eyes to the joys of Japanese animation in the late ‘90s. This sci-fi western noir takes all the familiar tropes of an old cowboy show — bounty hunters chasing bad guys, drifting from town to town, fighting crime in a lawless wasteland — and launches them to the edge of the universe.

In a future where the Solar System has been colonized, and the human race is more divided than ever, the crew of the Bebop balance their fledgling bounty-hunting career with their heartbreaking personal struggles. Every episode is a dazzling intergalactic adventure, but it’s also a deeply touching human drama about fascinating, layered, sympathetic characters. Cowboy Bebop paved the way for every TV space western from Firefly to The Mandalorian.

After playing a hardware salesman-turned-reluctant sheriff in HBO’s Deadwood, Timothy Olyphant took his gun belt and his cowboy boots and swaggered 130-ish years into the future to play a very different kind of TV gunslinger in Justified. Although it’s set in the modern day, Justified is more of a traditional western than Deadwood was. Deadwood was brutal and bloody and painstakingly historically accurate, but Justified is a lighthearted action series about a U.S. Marshal chasing fugitives across the American frontier.

Justified was based on an Elmore Leonard short story, “Fire in the Hole,” and the dialogue has that Leonard-lite, Tarantinoan clip to it. Olyphant shares an endlessly riveting cat-and-mouse dynamic with his on-screen arch-nemesis Walton Goggins. In its first season, Justified started out as a traditional case-of-the-week show, but it became a more serialized drama starting in season 2, and then it was off to the races.

Vince Gilligan changed the way a story could be told on television with Breaking Bad. David Chase had introduced the notion of the TV antihero in The Sopranos, but Gilligan took it a step further and used the long-form medium of television to chronicle a character’s gradual turn from hero to villain. Tony Soprano is the same character in the pilot episode that he is in the series finale, but Walter White slowly transforms from a timid suburban family man into a ruthless drug lord with plenty of blood on his hands, and Bryan Cranston played every step of that dramatic journey pitch-perfectly.

Gilligan told that story of moral downfall through the lens of a contemporary western. He set Breaking Bad on the dusty streets and sun-scorched deserts of Albuquerque; he put an armed standoff in every other episode; he even had Walt and his cohorts rob a train in the final season.

John Wick Faces Off Against Ballerina in This Invasion Thriller Officially Leaving HBO Max

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

He has covered everything from Marvel to the Oscars, and Marvel at the Oscars. He also writes obsessively about the box office, charting the many hits and misses that are released weekly, and how their commercial performance shapes public perception. In his time at Collider, he has also helped drive diversity by writing stories about the multiple Indian film industries, with a goal of introducing audiences to a whole new world of cinema.

Years before they shared the screen in From the World of John Wick: Ballerina, Ana de Armas and Keanu Reeves starred together in an erotic exploitation movie directed by Eli Roth. A friend and collaborator of Quentin Tarantino, Roth had already established his genre credentials with the horror film Hostel and the cannibal survival thriller The Green Inferno — both are regarded as some of the most transgressive movies made in mainstream Hollywood. Roth's home invasion thriller with De Armas and Reeves wasn't as edgy, although it was still shocking enough to remain in its niche. The film in question is currently streaming on HBO Max in the United States, giving fans of genre cinema a great opportunity to check it out.

It won't, however, be available for much longer. The movie was released a year after the first John Wick, which emerged as a surprise hit and reinvigorated Reeves' career as an action star. The rest is history, with the John Wick franchise having generated more than $1 billion in global box-office revenue in the decade since. Reeves is set to return as the titular assassin in a fifth film, following his extended cameo in Ballerina, which was released last year.

Ballerina marked De Armas' first time leading a potential franchise-starter of her own, after having delivered memorable performances in hits such as Knives Out, No Time to Die, and the Oscar-nominated Blonde. Despite mostly positive reviews, Ballerina underperformed at the box office, grossing around $140 million against a reported budget of $90 million. De Armas wasn't a household name back in 2015, when she first worked with Reeves in the home invasion thriller.

Four franchises, four completely different ways of getting underneath your skin. This is the one built out of your particular fear, why it works so well on you, and what it would leave behind.

Your nightmare does not wait outside the door. It waits for you to close your eyes, which you are going to have to do eventually, because nobody has ever won that particular argument.

Your nightmare is not really the doll. It is the face of the person you tell, and the way they decide, very kindly, that you have imagined all of it.

Your nightmare has your number, knows your name, and has almost certainly been inside your house before. The worst part is that you already know them.

Your nightmare is not really about you. It is about the people asleep down the hall, and the feeling that whatever is here is not interested in leaving.

We're talking, of course, about Knock Knock. The movie follows two young women who show up at the doorstep of an architect (Reeves) and coerce him into committing a sexual transgression. Soon, the architect finds himself at their mercy, as they hold him hostage in his house. The movie received mixed reviews and is now sitting at a 37% score on Rotten Tomatoes, where the consensus reads, "Knock Knock brings a lot of talent to bear on its satirical approach to torture horror, but not effectively enough to overcome its repetitive story or misguidedly campy tone."

Audiences were apparently not on the same page either, awarding it a poor 19% score on the aggregator website. Knock Knock grossed around $6 million at the box office against a reported budget of $10 million. Reeves will next star in another survival thriller — Shiver, directed by Deadpool filmmaker Tim Miller. You can watch Knock Knock on HBO Max until December 1, and stay tuned to Collider for more updates.

A devoted husband and father helps two stranded young women who knock on his door, but his kind gesture turns into a dangerous seduction and a deadly game of cat and mouse.

What are the limitations of eGPUs vs onboard graphics cards for laptops?

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Travel  |  Read Source →

An eGPU can bring desktop-class graphics to a laptop, but cables, bandwidth limits and extra costs can eat into the advantage.

Connecting a desktop graphics card to your laptop seems like a neat solution to one of the oldest problems in portable computing. Use the thin machine for work and travel, and connect to a much more powerful GPU when you get home. An external GPU (eGPU) can do just that, but all that extra graphics power still has to travel through Thunderbolt or USB4 instead of the direct internal connection used by a built-in laptop GPU. That bandwidth bottleneck can eat into the desktop card's advantage, and the cable it's connected to holds sway as well.

In this comparison, "onboard" primarily refers to a dedicated GPU built into the laptop, not the integrated graphics in the laptop's processor. Even when a laptop GPU shares its name with a desktop GPU, the hardware can be vastly different. The RTX 5090 Laptop GPU features 10,496 CUDA cores, 24GB of GDDR7 memory, and a GPU subsystem power range of 95W to 150W. Meanwhile, you'll find 21,760 CUDA cores and 32GB of GDDR7 memory on NVIDIA's desktop RTX 5090. That's a significant gap before even considering bottlenecks, like Thunderbolt.

A modern eGPU typically communicates with the laptop via Thunderbolt or USB4, rather than directly on its PCIe bus, and that's where some of the desktop card's edge is lost. A built-in laptop GPU uses an internal PCIe connection instead.

Thunderbolt 4 has 40 Gbps of overall bandwidth and requires 32 Gbps of PCIe data throughput, while Thunderbolt 5 upgrades the base speed to 80 Gbps and doubles that PCIe requirement to 64 Gbps. Internal laptop GPUs can use wider multi-lane PCIe connections, depending on the laptop's design, giving them more room to move data without the Thunderbolt bottleneck.

That bottleneck is more noticeable when it comes to a powerful desktop card. NVIDIA's desktop RTX 5090 has 1,792GB/s of memory bandwidth and a 575W total graphics power rating, compared with 896GB/s and a 95W to 150W GPU subsystem power range for the RTX 5090 Laptop GPU. Those aren't PCIe bandwidth figures, but they show how much more raw hardware the desktop card brings before the external connection starts holding it back.

Thunderbolt 5 also has a 120 Gbps Bandwidth Boost mode, but that isn't 120 Gbps of PCIe bandwidth for the GPU. Intel says PCIe data throughput remains at 64 Gbps. While USB4 can also support PCIe traffic, a USB-C-shaped port alone doesn't guarantee eGPU compatibility. The host hardware needs the right support, and the enclosure, drivers, GPU and power requirements must all cooperate (because apparently one cable was getting too easy).

Using the laptop's built-in display can add another penalty because rendered frames may have to travel back through the same limited connection. This is why some brands suggest connecting an external monitor directly to the eGPU for better performance. There's no universal performance-loss percentage, since the GPU, laptop, connection, resolution and game all affect the result.

A laptop GPU begins with less raw hardware, but it doesn't have to deal with most of this plumbing. It operates within the laptop's cooling and power constraints, doesn't have to send rendered frames back over an external cable, and remains in the laptop when you pick it up. The portability benefit is more like a suggestion with a traditional desktop-card eGPU. You now have an enclosure, a desktop GPU, and maybe an ATX power supply on the desk, while a big card can add size and noise to the mix.

Let's not forget to talk about the cost associated with eGPUs. As an example, Razer's Thunderbolt 5 Core X V2 supports full-size NVIDIA and AMD desktop cards up to four slots wide. The enclosure also has its own 120mm cooling fan, but it doesn't come with a GPU or even the necessary ATX power supply to keep it running. Those would be added expenses on top of approximately $350 for the enclosure itself.

That math gets considerably friendlier if you have a suitable GPU sitting around after a desktop upgrade. At that point, the enclosure could significantly upgrade your laptop without buying another computer. It's more difficult to justify purchasing a high-end desktop card specifically for an eGPU, especially since you're paying for all that power and then sending it through a cable that might not allow you to use it all.

Premier League: reduced to ten, Manchester United takes on water against Chelsea and sinks into crisis

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Opening shock of the 9th day of the Premier League, Chelsea and Manchester United met this Saturday in a delicate context. The Red Devils, in the midst of a crisis, were in 16th place in the standings, while the Blues, fifth, suffered a heavy home defeat against Tottenham. For this poster, Xabi Alonso had to deal with numerous absences: Maxence Lacroix, injured in the muscle in the middle of the week, João Pedro forfeited and Jordan Henderson, ill, were absent. The technician also left Malo Gusto on the bench, preferring Palestra, and aligned an attacking trio made up of Pedro Neto, Estêvão and Morgan Rogers, with Cole Palmer in support. For its part, Manchester United found its captain Bruno Fernandes, starter after the controversy with Michael Carrick. The Portuguese had openly criticized his coach’s game plan after the defeat against Como (4-1) in the Champions League. In difficulty in recent weeks, Frenchman Leny Yoro occupied the right flank of the Mancunian defense.

And the first period was particularly lively. Amad Diallo was very active and put Wesley Fofana in great difficulty lacking confidence after his missed penalty in the League Cup against Liverpool. For the visitors, Bruno Fernandes missed his face-to-face with Penders at the start of the match, before Matheus Cunha thought he could open the scoring from a corner in the 36th minute. But his goal was disallowed for offside. Chelsea also thought they could take the lead through Estêvão, whose goal was canceled for the same reason. Overall dominant, the Mancuniens imposed their rhythm, but the two teams returned to the locker room with a goalless draw.

But Morgan Rogers punished United as soon as they returned from the locker room. On a high recovery, the English striker immediately went deep and rushed into the Mancunian area. Between Leny Yoro and Harry Maguire, the hole left in the center was gaping: Rogers only had to slip into this space to present himself against Senne Lammens and steal his ball perfectly (1-0, 49th). An action which highlighted the enormous defensive shortcomings of the Red Devils, far too passive and disorganized. Lisandro Martínez perfectly symbolized the defensive wreck of Manchester United. The Argentine defender first received a yellow card in the 59th minute after a foul on Morgan Rogers, who was heading towards the goal. But the nightmare was only just beginning.

After intervention by the VAR, the referee gave him a second warning, synonymous with expulsion, and designated the penalty spot. Manchester United, however, avoided sinking further thanks to Harry Maguire. In the 67th minute, the English defender saved his team from 3-0 by beating Cole Palmer, who was alone in front of the goal, on a perfect serve from Estêvão. A slight burst of pride. In the 72nd minute, Chelsea thought they scored a third goal thanks to a superb right-footed shot from their Brazilian winger. But, as in his first attempt, the goal was ultimately disallowed for an offside position. Manchester United once again did very well and avoided seeing the gap widen further.

However, a final thrill ran through Stamford Bridge in the 93rd minute, when Mbeumo saw his powerful volley crash into the bar, a symbol of Mancunian’s lack of success. Chelsea then managed their advantage perfectly and this defeat could cost Manchester United dearly. After the humiliation suffered against Bournemouth (3-0), this new setback could well be one too many for the Mancunian leaders. Michael Carrick’s future now appears more uncertain than ever. The Red Devils have not won a single match since their success against Sabah on September 10 in the Champions League. In the rankings, Chelsea have performed well and climbed to fourth place, while Manchester United remain stuck in 16th place. Worse still, depending on the other results of this day, Carrick’s men could find themselves last in the championship. The crisis is total at Old Trafford.

Ralph Lauren: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Pulling back from lower-tier doors trades short-term volume for brand equity, a tradeoff the article's third takeaway leaves unresolved.

On its second-quarter earnings call, held on Nov. 5, 2026, Ralph Lauren (RL -1.15%) announced that it raised full-year guidance after quarterly revenue and profit beat internal targets. The apparel maker is expanding margins by selling more goods at full price and building its luxury presence across global markets. That pricing power protects profitability even when store traffic slows in regions such as Europe. Three things management said stand out.

Asia has become the primary growth engine for Ralph Lauren, driven by direct-to-consumer (DTC) sales, which include company-owned stores and websites. Revenue in China rose by over 35% as management expanded its retail ecosystem in top cities.

Ralph Lauren increased its Average Unit Retail (AUR), the average price paid per item, by 9% during the quarter. That pricing power helped boost adjusted gross margin, the percentage of revenue kept after direct costs, to 70%.

To protect its premium positioning, Ralph Lauren is pulling back from off-price channels, which sell excess stock at deep discounts. Management is intentionally exiting lower-tier department stores to focus on higher-margin, full-price sales.

Marketing investments reached 8.6% of sales as Ralph Lauren funded major campaign activations. Brand storytelling drives customer acquisition across channels, and management intends to maintain high reinvestment levels alongside margin growth.

Pricing discipline, retail channel expansion in Asia, and direct customer engagement anchor future value as management navigates traffic headwinds in Europe.

What A Road Sign With A Black 'Y' Means (And Other Letters, Too)

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

We have covered all manner of weird road signs on Jalopnik before, including some seriously goofy ones. While oddities from around the globe are fun to explore and explain, it is a little more relevant to touch on road signs that crop up on U.S. soil. Some of these will no doubt be familiar, but others may not, so there is good reason to delve into them and provide a quick explainer. The black "Y" sign was the one that initially piqued our interest, which, by the way, highlights that a Y-shaped intersection is to be found up ahead. That led us to look at other weird letter- and symbol-focused road signs drivers may come across.

The obvious candidates are "P" for parking areas and "H" that marks hospitals, which are universally understood, but throughout this article, we will also explore what those weird "T" or upturned "U" signs mean, in addition to winding "S" and plus-symbol signs. That includes where drivers are most likely to find them and how best to react when they do. Listed in no particular order, here are our picks of eight letter- or symbol-focused road signs that we thought are worth exploring in a bit more detail.

As already explained briefly above, a sign that depicts a capitalized "Y" is there to let drivers know of a Y-shaped three-way intersection or a forked road ahead. The sign will typically be a diamond shape with a yellow background, which means it's a simple warning sign alerting drivers that the road splits. The choices of where to go are obvious — either left or right — but what might be less obvious is how the right of way is determined in a three-way intersection that isn't controlled by any other signs.

The general rule is that drivers must yield to the car on the right. Then there's always the first-to-arrive rule, which works as you would intend — the first to arrive at the split goes first. Of course, some drivers will assume right of way when it is not deserved, or perhaps they just do not see the other car(s). In such instances, it's best to yield to the other vehicle even if you believe you should have the right of way to ensure everyone gets past the intersection without incident.

When a road splits like this, it pays to be especially cautious. Drivers may attempt to dart from one lane to the next when correcting a last-minute wrong decision, plus motorbikes or cyclists could be hiding in your blind spots, so try to ensure you have sufficient space around you if possible. The NHTSA highlights that roughly one third of all traffic fatalities occur at signposted intersections, so doubling down on cautiousness here is never a bad idea, no matter how simple it may first appear.

Surprise surprise, the letter "T" on a road sign warns of a T-shaped intersection — we would hope that you managed to work that one out. The road you are traveling on is about to end and merge with another. Once again, this is a classic warning sign finished in a yellow and black colorway, on a diamond-shaped sign that lets drivers know something's coming up and they will have to act accordingly.

In this instance, drivers must yield to traffic on the intersecting road — they don't have the right of way here — before heading either left or right. There will usually be a stop or yield sign prior to the intersection itself, and to avoid any frustrating bumps with other drivers making late decisions, it's best to get into the required lane early rather than last-minute. Thankfully, of the deadliest intersections on U.S. roads, not many are T-shaped, but the same rules as above apply here. Give yourself space, keep an eye on your blind spots, and don't rush. Oh, and don't forget to use your turn signals either, even if you are in a dedicated turn lane.

Again, we won't be handing out prizes to anyone who is able to correctly guess this one. A cross or "plus" symbol indicates that a crossroad is coming up, and just like the other aforementioned sign, this too will be depicted on a black and yellow diamond-shaped warning sign. Here, traffic can and likely will be coming from all directions, so it's imperative to take the time that's needed and approach it with care. Be aware that drivers on the opposite leg might try to cross ahead of you as you pull out, and be sure to check traffic from both left and right directions. As always, make sure to follow the instructions depicted on any regulatory signs posted in your direction, as these might detail give-way instructions.

There is a good case to make here in favor of the roundabout. In many ways, it is a far better design than the traditional intersection, with fewer conflict points, no left-turns across the flow of traffic, and lower speeds. Roundabouts might seem perplexing to some, but according to data from the Federal Highway Administration, they have been proven to reduce injury and fatal crashes by 76% and 90%, respectively, plus they are far more time-efficient than traffic signals, so perhaps road users ought to just get used to them.

OK, that's not strictly true. This sign, which isn't an "S" at all — it's just a winding arrow — signifies that a winding road is up around. In other words, the natural habitat for a Miata owner who's looking for some engaging roads to make the most of their drop-top weekend car. Of course, the sign isn't actually there to tell drivers that the road is a great place to go hooning. Instead, it lets them know the road ahead has several curves one after another and that they should approach that section of road with some caution.

In fact, these "S" signs will frequently be accompanied by another advisory speed sign that suggests the maximum safe speed for the section. Drivers would be wise to pay attention to these speeds, as around 25% of all fatal road incidents happen on a curved section of road, with common causes being cars either drifting off into trees, rocks, or other fixed objects, or indeed drifting across the center of the road and causing a head-on collision with other traffic.

Anyone who can't figure this one out should probably start taking notes here. An upturned U-shaped arrow crossed out in red, of course, means that U-turns are not permitted. Drivers should continue on, take a left or right turn if available, and find another suitable route. Exceptions will not be made for those in classic red-and-white Gran Torinos.

This is the first sign featured on our list that strays from that typical yellow and black warning sign colorway. Instead, this is a rectangular regulatory sign with a white background. These signs can crop up just about anywhere, but they can commonly be spotted in business districts, on narrow streets, or busier stretches of the nation's roads, such as on multi-lane highways and freeways.

For those who may question the legality of U-turns when this sign isn't present, drivers can usually perform such a move in these circumstances: They should be in the leftmost or center-left lane, signalling their intention, and keeping an eye out for traffic as per any other manoeuvre.

A sideways "T" sign indicates that a side road is coming up. Depending on which way the T is oriented, the sign highlights whether the side road is on the right or the left. If the sign instead looks like an F or an E, but with an extended top or bottom, this means that two side roads are coming up on the right-hand side.

Specifically, this sign will appear when the side roads on the right are particularly close to one another. The hazard this poses to drivers on the major road is quite obvious; they should be wary of traffic entering from the right, and should be prepared to slow down. The sign itself is a typical diamond-shaped yellow-and-black warning sign, which does not indicate right of way. Typically, those on the major road will have the right of way, while those on the indicated side roads will have to yield to traffic on the major road.

The sign for a roundabout shows three arrows forming a circle, and once again, it will be a yellow-and-black diamond-shaped warning sign. Roundabouts always run in a counter-clockwise direction in North America. They can have numerous lanes and are an alternative to crossroads. Here, the right of way rules are simple and uniform: Drivers must always give way to traffic on the left — in other words, the cars that are already on the roundabout.

Getting the right lane is crucial for avoiding accidents; the leftmost lane is used for turning left, whereas the rightmost lanes are used for turning right, and both can be used for going straight on. These are the rules on multi-lane roundabouts unless signposted otherwise — this way, traffic in either lane does not cross unnecessarily.

As for whether drivers need to use turn signals on a roundabout or not, the answer is yes. It's never a bad idea to let other drivers know where you are going; even if you are comfortable in your own abilities to get across safely, it's best practice to help other motorists out by indicating to them your next intended move.

Boy Meets World’s Danielle Fishel Reveals One Topanga-Related Regret, And I Completely Understand

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

A Full House Actor Had ‘Too Big A Pack’ To Be Wearing Biker Shorts In One Kimmy Gibbler Scene

Danica McKellar Wants You To Know She Didn’t LEAVE Hallmark. How She Feels

I Don’t Know How I Feel About ‘The Rachel’ Making A Comeback, But Jennifer Aniston Has Thoughts

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Danielle Fishel charmed audiences for seven seasons while playing the TGIF fan-favorite character, Topanga Lawrence in Boy Meets World. Not only was the character notable for being involved in a TV relationship that defined goals, but Topanga was also loved for being smart and true to herself. While Fishel has taken great pride in her BMW work, she recently mentioned one regret related to her character that, as a fan, I completely sympathize with.

At the beginning of the ABC sitcom, Topanga was introduced as Cory Matthews’ hippie-like classmate whose otherworldly views always sparked great comedic moments. But by the second season, th flower child had an extreme personality change, where she transitioned to an extremely motivated student and later the object of Cory’s affections by Season 3.

In Fishel, Rider Strong, and Will Friedle’s new book, Book Meets World: The Definitive Inside Story of the Hit Sitcom Boy Meets World (via People), she got real about her character's evolution. She explained how she felt about Topanga's trajectory at the time compared to how she feels now:

Teenage Danielle was more comfortable being the more mainstream Topanga because they wrote her more like me. So I probably didn’t have a problem with it then. Now I think, what a gift to be given a character that has such a sharply defined POV, to be so unique and to have been such a good role model. I knew that she was special. But then, when it changed, and she kind of evolved, I don’t think I necessarily knew to mourn it.

I get what the Dancing with the Stars contestant is talking about. When Fishel previously spoke about Topanga’s personality shift, she admitted that she didn’t even notice the change at the time. Looking back, it’s true that the show was focusing a lot on Topanga’s evolving look, like in Season 2 when Shawn was staring at her chest or Cory fixated so much on her beauty in Season 3. I truly believe Topanga’s complete change was when she cut her trademark long hair and started to look more like many other woman of the ‘90s.

While no one formally told the Dish host that her character would deviate from her weird girl persona, she said she remembered hearing a conversation from the crew about wanting to make Topanga “more normal” for Cory. Yet, even in the Season 6 episode, "Resurrection," Cory talked about missing the old Topanga. I feel like Cory would have grown to like the more off-beat version of his eventual partner.

Something that's interesting is hearing Fishel mention that as a teen, she didn't really mind her character being more streamlined and fitting her own personality. She may not have mourned the transition then, but she certainly does now:

Now I see it, and I so mourn it. I would have loved even little flashbacks to it.

That would have been great. Fans see a flashback scene in “Resurrection” of when Topanga put lipstick all over her face but, in the later seasons, I would have liked to have seen Fishel's character retain traits of who she was early on.

At the same time, we can be thankful that no matter who Topanga grew up to be, she remained throughout the series in general. Some may not be aware that she was originally written to be a recurring character. As a result of her sticking around, fans got to see her transform into a confident young woman, who even became an incredible lawyer, wife, and mother in the spinoff series, Girl Meets World.

While Danielle Fishel now regrets having leaned into Topanga’s personality change, I'd argue that her performance on the show still deserves praise. And, of course, we can love the iconic character for who she was when she was first introduced as well as who she grew into by the show’s series finale. You can watch Topanga’s Boy Meets World and Girl Meets World journeys by streaming the sitcoms with a Disney+ subscription.

Just your average South Floridian cinephile who believes the pen is mightier than the sword.

Meryl Streep, Jon Hamm, Dave Franco Brought Comedy To ‘Hoppers’, Director Daniel Chong Says – Contenders L.A.

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Pixar animated film Hoppers features an eclectic voice cast, from Meryl Streep and Jon Hamm to Dave Franco and Bobby Moynihan. Whatever their background, director Daniel Chong wanted funny people. At Contenders Film, Chong said comedy drove the casting.

“Comedy was the No. 1 thing we wanted as casting,” Chong said. “[Hamm’s] post-Mad Men career has been a lot of comedy. That guy is not afraid to look stupid. We definitely put him through it in the movie. Meryl was also down for jokes. She came in with that really weird voice. She came in hot with that voice and we were like okay, here we go.”

Hamm plays the mayor who is approving the razing of a glade. Meryl Streep plays the insect queen. In the film, young Mabel (Piper Curda), uses Hopper technology to control a robot beaver. As a beaver, she rallies the animals to save the glade. Watch on Deadline

Franco plays Streep’s son and worked hard to keep up.

“With Dave, we also put him through the ringer because that is a very high octane performance,” Chong said. “He has to go to 11 every line. He was sweating after every take.”

Curda joined Chong and producer Nicole Paradis Grindle at Contenders. Curda was a TikToker, Chong discovered.

“That’s not why I made TikTok,” Curda said. “I was bored. Now I’m here.”

Mabel is a young environmentalist. Curda hopes her passion inspires young viewers.

“What’s so cool about Mabel is she’s not shy about really caring and also at the same time, she’s incredibly cool,” Curda said. “it’s such a cool takeaway, especially for a young person, to be like it’s cool to care. You can care a lot about something maybe most people don’t care about and still be awesome. I think it’s really cool to care.”

Grindle acknowledged there is a message to Hoppers. Comedy helps them deliver it, as she quoted another Disney classic.

“A spoonful of sugar makes the medicine go down,” Grindle said. “We’re all about the sugar. It’s more meaningful when it’s rooted in something deeper. You need to have that kind of emotional catharsis to come out of the movie.”

Regis: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Regis's guest traffic fell 1.7% while franchisees closed 34 salons, leaving the turnaround's success tied to whether it can stop that erosion.

On its first quarter of fiscal 2027 earnings call, held on Nov. 5, 2026, Regis (RGS -1.38%) detailed its plan to boost guest traffic and stabilize franchisee operations. The salon franchisor is navigating customer traffic declines while managing debt reduction and store closures. Three things management said stand out.

Supercuts system traffic fell 1.7% during the quarter, making guest acquisition and retention a central priority. Management is expanding Choice Booking, an online scheduling tool that allows digital appointments alongside traditional walk-ins, after test locations saw higher customer visits and fewer online booking errors.

Management evaluated multiple refinancing options for its $121.1 million outstanding debt, including bank loans and private credit financing. Potential lenders requested additional quarters of operational progress before agreeing to new terms, prompting the company to delay refinancing until its prepayment penalty drops in June 2027.

Franchisees closed 34 locations in the first quarter, threatening recurring fee streams. To mitigate revenue erosion, management launched an artificial intelligence (AI) health dashboard to identify underperforming locations early and facilitate salon transfers to stronger operators.

Operational turnarounds are underway across core salon brands while the company maintains positive cash flow. Management is focusing on franchise resales, localized marketing, and expanded digital booking tools to stabilize system revenue.

Long-term outcomes depend on whether operational changes can curb guest traffic erosion and reduce salon closures. Aligning franchisee execution with modernized tools remains critical to building salon-level profitability.

Trans Catalina Day 2: Views for Days

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Food  |  Read Source →

Despite being a late sleeper at home, I always seem to wake up early on trail. Today, I wake up before 6 am, but I force myself to sleep until 6:30. My campsite is just under 2 miles from Airport in the Sky, Catalina’s small airport. It has a small shop and offers breakfast, but it doesn’t open until 8:30 am.

I take my time packing up, enjoying the cool morning temperatures, finally heading out around 8:15 am. I am the first person in camp to leave.

Almost immediately I encounter my first confirmed sighting of the Catalina Quail. Two walk down the road in front of me as I climb the road out of Black Jack. I see countless quails the rest of the hike, almost always in pairs. It seems it may be mating season!

The trail follows a road before heading sharply downhill on singletrack. There seems to be no one else out enjoying the cool morning hiking hours. It is beautiful, the sun glinting off the ocean and the dew.

The trail dives into a valley before climbing to the airport. The valley is lush and green, and I even encounter a small, stagnant stream at the bottom — remarkable given the dry reputation of the trail.

What goes down must go up, though. After reaching the bottom of the valley, the trail immediately twists up towards the airport. I could see the airport from the top of the valley before descending, and it looks barely closer. The sun has come out and is beating down, even this early. This side of the valley is more exposed, with no trees and minimal foliage. The descent actually had a few trees, but it’s clear this ascent is closer to what the rest of the day will look like.

Airport in the Sky taunting me as I climb toward it.

Breakfast “sandie” – eggs, bacon, cheddar, avocado on sourdough.

I sit on the porch and enjoy the chance cool down, rehydrate, and fuel up. More and more people arrive on planes and from trail as the morning continues. It seems less crowded if you arrive for breakfast than for lunch. The line is much longer when I leave an hour and half after arriving.

My only regret is that I never got to meet the airport’s resident black cat.

As I head out, the sun is blazing in the middle of the sky, no cloud in sight. Today’s hike is much shorter and easier than yesterday’s, but the temperature is much warmer. As I scope out my path ahead, I see few trees and lots of road walking. It’s going to be a warm afternoon.

The trail alternates between singletrack and dirt road. I spend a couple of miles cruising along the wide, shadeless road before diving back into the landscape, but the road walk is not unpleasant. The views are perpetual and the track is relatively quiet. I quickly realize, though, that with minimal tree coverage, bathroom breaks require creativity or a lack of modesty — or both. The hillside often steeply climbs away from the road, making it even more difficult.

A lovely blooming cactus flower from along the side of the trail.

As I leave the road for singletrack, I notice movement on the side of trail. A small lizard freezes as I approach. I’ve noticed two distinct types of small lizards, but this one is my favorite. It has rainbow coloring, appearing almost to shimmer in the sun. I believe it is the common side-blotched lizard. I have already seen quite a few of them, and will see many more before the end of my hike.

The side-blotched lizard, known for the male’s throat colors indicating their mating style.

I continue to dodge more lizards under foot as I climb up a crest, and immediately my eyes are drawn forward. The trail winds along the very top of ridge before beginning the descent into Little Harbor. It’s rare for me to experience a trail on a ridge like this, and it takes my breath away.

The ridge walk is lovely, but the constant sun starts to make me weary. The temperature keeps increasing, no clouds or shade breaks to be found. I stop to sip my electrolytes and re-apply sunblock. Just ahead, at the bottom of this hill, I see a semi-shaded bench at a trail junction. It becomes all I focus on, my entire motivation for the remaining ridge walk and descent. Get. To. Shade.

The palm trees come into view, making me both smile (camp is near!) and chuckle (palm trees are not native). Mostly, I am thrilled at the view of trees. Trees mean shade. Shade means relief.

Even so, I stop and take in the view. It really is a picturesque sight.

Once at the campground, I head for Campsite #10, which turns out to be at the very front — right by the beach. Talk about incredible luck. When I reserved it, I was not sure what I was getting. A giant palm tree provides much needed shade over a fire ring and picnic table. A stream inlet burbles past, while straight ahead is the harbor itself.

I spend the rest of the afternoon exploring the camp and the beach. There is a water spigot in the next camp over, just like last night — though this isn’t typical. A row of porta potties on the road just outside the campsites serve as the toilets. Not my favorite, but at least there is a hand washing station!

I run into some fellow hikers I met at the shaded bench earlier. With wide eyes, one asks me, “Did you see the bison as you walked into camp?” Surprised, I reply that I did not. Apparently, it was lounging under a tree next to the trail into camp and I walked right past it. It must have blended with the dirt, obscured in the shade.

Despite missing the bison sighting, I still see quite a few new creatures throughout the afternoon and evening. I encounter more more Catalina quail as I walk around – click here for the video: Bobbing Catalina Quail. I see several Brown Pelicans diving for food near the shore.

What I believe to be a brown pelican hunting in the slough next to my campsite.

The real gem of wildlife for the evening, though, is the Catalina Fox. I say hi to the Isabellas at their campsite, and as we chat, a fox scurries past just behind the campsite. It slows and looks longingly at the picnic table, clearly familiar with human food, before finally disappearing into the bushes.

The Catalina Fox, staring longingly at our food. It looks a little like a stray dog.

Little Harbor is absolutely lovely. After dinner, I wander onto the beach to enjoy the sunset. The temperatures have finally cooled down, thanks in part to the cool ocean breeze. I enjoy it while I can.

Tomorrow is the day I am most nervous about. The temperatures are supposed to be highest tomorrow, and the official trail has a brutally steep climb and descent with zero shade, which I will face in the full afternoon sun. Somehow, though, I don’t feel too daunted. I feel excited, rejuvenated by my lovely evening. I knew the trail was short, but tomorrow is my last full day on trail. I can’t believe how fast it has flown by.

I crawl into my tent early, ready to get an early start to beat the heat tomorrow. Perhaps tomorrow is the day for my bison sighting!

Miles Hiked: 8.5 Elevation Gain/Lost: 1125 ft gained/ 2600 ft lost Start/End: Black Jack Campground to Little Harbor Campground Lowlight: Heat Highlight: The amazing beach campsite at Little Harbor Bison Count: 0 (near miss!) Fox Count: 1

The buyer pays the same price as they would otherwise, and your purchase helps to support The Trek's ongoing goal to serve you quality backpacking advice and information. Thanks for your support!

Hi! I am Ruth, or Sailor. I hiked the Colorado Trail in 2022-2023. In 2025, I completed the Loowit Trail, Timberline Trail, and Wonderland Trail. This year, I am hiking the Trans-Catalina trail and the Wonderland Trail (again!). Off-trail, I am a professional classical singer/voice teacher, though you might not believe that if you hear me singing to ward off bears!

Cars.com: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Self-service listings target smaller dealers and budget inventory, but the article leaves open whether that growth can coexist with the margin gains.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 5, 2026, Cars.com (CARS -1.09%) delivered record quarterly adjusted EBITDA, a measure of core operational profitability, despite flat top-line growth. Long-term investors should note how cost discipline and new digital tools are expanding operating leverage while filling inventory gaps. Three things management said stand out.

Management introduced a no-fee starter package that lets smaller dealers list vehicles without manual onboarding assistance. The initiative expands platform inventory in the under-$20,000 price range, attracting budget-conscious shoppers and creating future upsell opportunities.

Organizational changes made earlier in the year removed management layers and reduced duplicated work across business units. These cost-containment efforts helped push the adjusted EBITDA margin, which measures profitability before interest, tax, and accounting charges, to nearly 31%.

The Carson artificial intelligence shopping assistant reached over 1 million monthly users as conversational search behavior expanded on the platform. Management shifted marketing spend toward high-intent shoppers, driving roughly double-digit growth in sales leads despite lower overall site traffic.

Operating leverage is expanding across the core marketplace as organizational restructuring takes hold. The business relies on high customer retention, direct integrations with artificial intelligence traffic, and broad dealer coverage to maintain its market footprint while monitoring pricing discipline and subscription adoption rates across its platform.

Management plans to extend its operational framework to underperforming website solutions while maintaining share repurchases.

5 Amazing Western Revenge Movies You Can Watch For Free

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

For those who enjoy watching hardened gunslingers shooting their way to revenge, there are plenty of worthwhile options that can be streamed for free. Tubi, a streaming service known for not requiring a paid subscription to watch its content, hosts a vast library of Western movies.

Vengeance is one of the most popular themes of Western films. It's the driving force of so many stories in the genre. Because of how common it can be, a long list of these Westerns proved to be rather forgettable, somewhat standard entries into the genre. Others, however, stood out from the crowd to join the ranks of the greatest Westerns of all time, a distinction earned by the likes of Unforgiven, Hang 'Em High, The Searchers, and For a Few Dollars More.

A few movies that belong in that same category can currently be streamed for free, thanks to Tubi. Beyond that, the streaming service also offers a number of lesser-known - but incredibly entertaining - Western movies that cast the spotlight on bitter gunslingers with a score to settle.

Released in 2003, Open Range stars Western icons Kevin Costner and Robert Duvall as aging ranchers who resist when a gang of thugs led by an unscrupulous cattle baron tries to bully them off the land in the Montana territory. Open Range starts out as a story of resistance against a powerful force in a Western town, but when the villains of the film ultimately take a step too far, it evolves into a brutal tale of revenge.

The mysterious past of Kevin Costner's character comes into play here, with his skills as a gunfighter being key to their efforts to fight back. Their determination to not only hold their own ground, but also get revenge for their fallen culminates in a strikingly gruesome grand finale. Unlike many of the Westerns of the previous era, Open Range pulls no punches when it comes to showing its characters dying in grisly fashion. But the violence is never gratitious; Open Range successfully presents itself as a dark but appropriately realistic take on life in the American Wild West.

With its gritty take on the Western formula and outstanding performances from its two lead actors, Open Range has a well-deserved reputation as one of the greatest Westerns of the 21st century thus far.

The Forgotten Pistolero is an underrated 1969 Spaghetti Western that places Leonard Mann in the role of Sebastian, a man who grew up with no knowledge of his true history. After getting dragged into a conflict with a pair of scheming criminals and their cronies, Sebastian learns the tragic details of his past, and the identies of those responsible. Teaming up with his friend, Rafael, Sebastian goes on a mission of revenge to get justice for his father and save his sister.

A big part of what makes The Forgotten Pistolero's revenge tale so interesting is the target of his crusade; Sebastian's own mother is one of the two main villains of the movie, setting up an emotional confrontation at the end of the story where the hero gets a chance to make her and her former lover pay for their crimes. If the plot sounds familiar, that's because The Forgotten Pistolero is essentially a loose adaptation of the Greek myth of Orestes, though done in the format of a Spaghetti Western. It's an unorthodox combination, but the concept makes for a compelling revenge film.

In 1971, Western heavyweight Burt Lancaster starred in Valdez is Coming. Lancaster plays a lawman named Valdez, who was deceived into killing someone in the movie's opening act. Shortly afterward, the man who perpetrated the deception used the power he holds in the town to have Valdez painfully humiliated and dragged out into the desert.

Unbeknownst to the villain, Burt Lancaster's Valdez is a seasoned sharpshooter, a background that is crucial to his plan of retaliation. Unwilling to forget what was done to him, Valdez meticulously hunts down his enemy's henchmen, one by one. Valdez's relentless killing spree continues until it builds into a dramatic showdown with his prime target.

Similar in some ways to Unforgiven, Valdez is Coming is a movie where the villains unwittingly invite disaster upon themselves by an old gunslinger they once deemed as a non-threat. Fear consumes them as Lancaster's character easily asserts control over the situation. He's on his own, but it's clearly the villains who have their backs against the wall.

For those who enjoy the one-man army aspect of both Western movies and modern-day revenge thrillers like John Wick, Valdez is Coming is a must-watch. And despite being in his late 50s at this point in his career, Burt Lancaster is still as imposing as ever, demonstrating his worth as one of the Western genre's best leading actors across its entire history in Hollywood.

Two years into the legendary run of the celebrated Western TV series Gunsmoke, the show's star - James Arness - played the lead role in Gun the Man Down, a significantly darker film set in the same genre. Rather than keeping the peace and maintaining law in order, Arness plays an outlaw with a serious grudge on his mind, and one whose vicious methods go a bit beyond the norm.

Gun the Man Down delivers a gritty take on the Western revenge trope that is likely to please fans of movies like Hang 'Em High and High Plains Drifter. In the movie, Arness' character is a criminal whose cohorts betrayed him and essentially left him holding the bag. But rather than simply exact revenge on them following his release from prison, he decides to drag out the process by terrorizing them emotionally, adding an interesting, psychological horror element to the Western.

In 1959's Ride Lonesome, Randolph Scott plays a bounty hunter named Ben Brigade, who captures a criminal (James Best) and sets out to deliver him to justice. But his efforts actually hide an ulterior motive; his real goal is to force out of hiding his prisoner's parter-in-crime, who is responsible for the death of Brigade's wife years earlier.

Ride Lonesome boasts a surprising amount of star power; in addition to having Randolph Scott in the lead role, the 1959 Western also includes bad guy roles for two of the Western genre's best villain actors in Lee Van Cleef and James Coburn, and naturally, neither disappoints. The sinister nature of Van Cleef's character only make Randolph Scott's hero even easier to root for.

Ride Lonesome is easily one of Randolph Scott's best Westerns, and that's saying a lot when taking into consideration that he was one of the most prolific and most popular actors associated with in the genre during the 1940s and 1950s.

Ewan McGregor’s ‘Jurassic Park’ Replacement Is Officially Taking Over Streaming

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

It's difficult to imagine a busier Hollywood star in 2026 than Anne Hathaway. Not only is she expecting her third child with her husband, Adam Shulman, and has been lined up to star alongside Tom Cruise in Day of Thunder 2, but she has also been part of an incredible five of the year's big-screen hits. 2026 began with Hathaway's quietest release, as she starred in the criminally underseen Mother Mary from A24, which has since become a streaming hit.

Then came one of her most anticipated performances of the year, as she rejoined Meryl Streep, Stanley Tucci, and Emily Blunt for The Devil Wears Prada 2, which was called "near-perfect" by Collider's own Taylor Gates in a glowing review. The $693 million global haul of The Devil Wears Prada 2 pales in comparison to Hathaway's next project, which ranks as her highest-grossing of all time. She starred as Penelope in Christopher Nolan's $1.8 billion-earning historical epic The Odyssey, stealing scenes and helping provide an emotional anchor for Matt Damon's King of Ithaca.

Any film released after The Odyssey was always likely to struggle under the shadow of its predecessor. This is what happened to Hathaway's next release of 2026, The End of Oak Street, as she joined Star Wars alum Ewan McGregor as their quiet suburban life is inexplicably transported into the jaws of prehistoric danger. Sadly, The End of Oak Street earned just $120 million against a reported budget of $85 million. However, since moving to streaming, it's been able to step out of The Odyssey's shadow. At the time of writing, The End of Oak Street ranks as one of the most-watched movies on Amazon in the world.

Following The End of Oak Street, Hathaway wrapped up a congested 2026 with the release of Verity, the latest in a quick run of Colleen Hoover adaptations, following It Ends with Us, Regretting You, and Reminders of Him. Although the film was accused of being "afraid to go too campy, creepy, or even overtly erotic with its visual styling and script choices," in a review for Collider by Carly Lane, Hoover fans supported it in the millions and turned Verity into a box office hit.
