11 gadgets under $10 that solve everyday problems

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

You don't have to spend much to fix little annoyances in everyday life.

We may receive a commission on purchases made from links.

Steve Jobs once said that customers don't know what they want until you show them. And he was right. But he was also lucky because he was showing people iPhones and not, say, rechargeable nose trimmers.

Of course, when Apple launched the iPhone in 2007, Amazon wasn't the global behemoth it's since become. Now there's virtually nothing Amazon doesn't sell, and a lot of it is dirt cheap. In the post-Bezos world, Jobsian marketing principles no longer apply. Customers still may not know what they want, but if it's cheap enough, they'll buy it anyway and then throw it out and buy something else.

Thus begins our quest to assemble a list of cheap gadgets that are worth every penny, which should come in handy around the shopping season. If you treat them right, these may last you for many holidays to come.

It's the middle of the night when you wake up and need to head to the en-suite bathroom. You snap on the light and you're blinded because it's too bright. Or worse, you wake up your spouse, who can't easily get back to sleep even with their favorite sleeping app.

This scenario may sound exaggerated, but if you've ever been there, you know it isn't. Or, to spin it back to Steve Jobs, you just don't realize what you need. In this case, it's a motion sensor light for your bathroom.

Decent motion-activated lights for around $10 that don't require any drilling come in two varieties. They're either battery-powered stick-ons like the Star-Spangled LED Puck Light ($8.99 for 5), or they plug right into a socket. The latter are more like smart night lights. The BN-Link Backlit Motion Sensor ($12.34 for 2), for example, has an auto mode that only activates with motion, plus a night mode that gives a dim light until activated.

In both cases, you get to see at night without waking anybody else up. And you're good to go for years because the bulbs are LEDs and use very little energy.

Those of us driving around without built-in infotainment displays in our cars have tried every so-called solution for mounting our phones: suction cups, clips, vent mounts and more. The more expensive and heartier the holder, the faster they wilt, with one exception: peel-off phone magnets.

This WixGear offering costs $6.99 and is simple to use. First, peel off the film on the back. Stick one on the back of your phone (or preferably your phone case), stick the other on your dashboard, and bring your phone close to snap them together. It shouldn't plop to the ground ever again.

A few caveats here. One, the stickiness of the adhesive makes this a permanent fix, so don't expect to move the magnets around once they're on. Two, we recommend finding a vertical position on your dashboard that's close enough to tap, but not too close to a vent. Extreme temperature changes might lead to your phone slipping, while heat blowing on it is never good.

And three, a phone stuck to your dashboard can easily become dangerous. Don't spend too much time playing with it; you could easily get distracted. Apple has also reported that magnets can influence photo quality in certain cases, so keep an eye on that. All said, for speaker phone calls, Google Maps or playing music in your car, peel-off magnets are a tested fix.

There are two types of people: those who are aware of keyboard gunk and those who have blissfully never observed it. If you don't make a habit of cleaning your keyboard, the first tool you're likely to reach for to remove gunk is a brush. You may, with much practice, reach a state of competency where you can finagle stray brush hairs into the cracks around individual keys and dislodge the detritus of your typing life that way. It works, but you can do better.

A handheld, mouse-sized vacuum cleaner may strike you as effete, and in a sense, it is. But for those who like a clean keyboard, a vacuum is quicker and much more efficient than hunting for crumbs with a brush.

Our recommendation also comes with a nozzle, brush and USB charging cable, amenities that nudge it a bit over the $10 mark. While you can find cheaper options, caveat emptor. Some of them look good but get horrible customer reviews. For best results, disconnect your keyboard from a desktop PC (or shut down your laptop) before you clean.

If you haven't kept up with other forms of computer cleaning, it's also worth learning how to clean your screen the right way.

Yes, it's just a lowly phone stand. But it could be a life-saver. If you doubt us, you've probably never had a last-minute Zoom interview where, in lieu of propping your iPhone up against a fruit bowl, you opted for a grainy, handheld shot that gave your potential employer a bold view of your inner nostrils. That isn't the only reason to invest in an inexpensive, versatile phone holder, however.

Modern versions of iOS and Android can turn your phone into a webcam, which is a great way to reuse an old smartphone. But unless you own a folding phone, you're back at square one.

A budget stand like the Nulaxy solves this problem neatly. Whatever stand you end up with, make sure it's universal so you don't have to buy one for everyone in the house. It should have a gap at the bottom to make room for the charging cable, and remain steady whether in landscape or portrait.

There is perhaps nothing more disturbing than a wax-bejeweled earbud. Actually, there is: having to send your dirty, malfunctioning bud back to wherever you purchased it for a diagnosis of "ear wax accumulation." The minds behind the $6.99 Hyashee earbud cleaning pen are on our wavelength here.

Hyashee's earbud cleaners are geared toward AirPods, though the flocking sponge and brush combo will get the job done for any other brand too. As an alternative, there are other universal options like the Midukit cleaner kit. At $9.99, this comes with everything from the Hyashee Cleaner Kit, plus blobs of purple putty and some brushes. Dirt sticks to the putty so you can get it out that way.

Investing a few dollars in an earbud cleaner could improve the longevity of your buds, which makes a difference if you own a quality pair of AirPods. It could also save you from the disgrace of having to send your grimy headphones to a horrified technician at Best Buy. This applies even more if you've got kids with earbuds of their own.

There always ends up being someone who appears less often in family photos because they're the one behind the camera. To restore balance to your family, consider a remote Bluetooth camera shutter. The ATUMTEK Wireless Camera Remote Shutter is a popular choice; it's $8.99 and comes as a two-pack. It should pair with your phone up to distances of ~30 feet, and is compatible with both Android and iOS.

The Zttopo Wireless Bluetooth Camera Shutter Remote is about the same price. Reviews for both hover around 4.5 stars on Amazon. Just be warned that you'll need a tripod (or at least a stand, as above) before you assemble your first full family portrait.

You might not even need to buy a new flash drive to make this work; rifle around in your desk drawer and if you get lucky, you'll unearth a dust-covered treasure from 2011. If you can't find one — or if this is a stocking stuffer for techy friends or relatives — you can always order something like a basic Sandisk 32GB USB 2.0 drive around $9.99.

Why would you want to turn a USB stick into a portable antivirus? Because it might be all you need to clean up an infected Windows machine without having to call the Geek Squad. There are plenty of other neat ways to use an old USB thumb drive aside from storage, but this is our favorite.

When an attack strikes, it can take over your OS, locking you out of your antivirus. When you boot up an antivirus from a separate disk (your USB drive), you can bypass the infected system and clean it from the outside. ESET SysRescue Live is one example that's bootable from a flash drive.

For many of us, the main duty of our Google Nest Hub or similar device is to serve as a bark-activated kitchen timer. "Hey Google, set a timer for 20 minutes!... Stop!"

Considering the expense, why not just buy a digital kitchen timer? They're a lot cheaper and you won't have to bark at Google ever again. This minimal DALSHABET timer kind of looks like a Google Nest Thermostat, which is to say, it looks pretty good. It's also magnetic, so it sticks to your refrigerator or any other magnetic surface in the kitchen. And it's cheap at $9.99, though it could drop a little depending on sales.

The only part you might have to get used to is setting it manually. But that's just a twist of the dial. Is this extra manual labor worth it? If you want to save money and avoid adding yet another sporadically obedient screen of dubious utility to your home, this little $10 dial should last you a while.

As with many aspects of digital hygiene, there are competing views on webcam covers, as well as various methods for physically blocking access to your computer's camera.

Former FBI director James Comey admitted to taping over his laptop's camera. Meta CEO and former human Mark Zuckerberg was photographed doing the same in 2016. If you're worried that your camera might accidentally pop on when you don't intend it to, a set of $8 BLOCKED vinyl webcam stickers might be useful. Keep in mind that an attack's goal is more likely to install malware or trick you into handing over credentials than to spy on you.

If you're on a Mac, the camera will never turn on without its accompanying green indicator light. You may also damage your screen if you slap on a traditional slide cover, since razor-thin laptop design doesn't allow room for them. That said, for work laptops or desktops you've had for a while, a vinyl sticker is low-cost protection against suddenly appearing at a team meeting in your pajamas.

If we ever calculated how much time we've lost, as a species, to USB-related inconveniences, we would drag USB's creator before the ICC. His name is Ajay Bhatt, by the way, and he's been hiding in broad daylight reaping the fruits of his evil genius since 1996.

Yes, it's just a cable. But oh, what havoc USB can wreak. The problems are endless, but the source of our grief is usually the same: we have the wrong connector for a device. Our machine could also feature the wrong port for the cable we're trying to use. Or, if we're on an Apple laptop, we probably don't have enough ports. In all three cases, we're stuck.

The usual solution is to buy an adapter, but that can still run into issues. Maybe the adapter only works for one situation, like USB-A to USB-C. Or we shell out way more for a multi-purpose hub that has ports handling a bunch of different connections, including HDMI. Fortunately, there's a middle route that costs a lot less: a USB adapter kit.

This 12-piece IANFAN USB kit, at $8.59, offers a bunch of adapters that should cover any USB compatibility issues you face. If you want a handier solution geared to USB-C compatibility, try the Povdite charging cable adapter kit, which comes with a case and its own charging cable. Whichever you choose, you'll be doing your part in the fight against uncontrolled cable sprawl.

Wireless charging pads present an interesting dilemma for the sustainability-conscious gadget shopper. Will replacing one accessory (a USB cable) with another (a charging pad) actually make your life easier and the planet better? Or will you just feed the yawning maw of your accessory drawer one more tasty meal?

A charging pad still needs to be powered by a cord, so it's not truly wireless. But once you plug it in, you won't have to worry about USB compatibility anymore, as long as your devices all support wireless charging. If you can find a quality charging pad that powers multiple devices, that's even better. But that can get tricky, especially when your budget is 10 dollars.

Cheaper charging pads also might not charge as quickly, as outputs vary. And one station might not be enough for a whole household. That said, if you want to test the waters, this NANAMI Fast Wireless Charger Pad costs $9.99. It works with Android phones, iPhones and even earbuds, with a maximum charging power of 15W.

Canada Goose: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Store traffic is the open question: the retail network is where the apparel gains either hold up or stall.

On its second quarter of fiscal 2027 earnings call, held on Nov. 5, 2026, Canada Goose (GOOS +2.17%) announced a 5% revenue increase to 274 million Canadian dollars while expanding its gross profit margins. Long-term investors need to evaluate whether the luxury outerwear maker can build a sustainable year-round business while addressing headwinds in retail store traffic. Three things management said stand out.

Management expanded gross margins through improved sourcing, manufacturing efficiency, and higher pricing, rather than relying on short-term external boosts. These operational upgrades helped expand gross margins even as retail network costs increased.

Expanding beyond traditional heavy parkas creates a balanced business model that reduces reliance on cold weather. Sales growth in lighter apparel and rainwear indicates that retail customers increasingly purchase these products from the brand year-round.

Lower physical store traffic dragged down direct-to-consumer (DTC) comparable sales, which measure performance at locations open for over a year. Better online sales and store conversion rates partially cushioned the drop, but store foot traffic remains a clear challenge.

Wholesale orders are expanding, and management projects steady progress toward its annual profitability targets. Operational upgrades and pricing discipline support long-term margins while leadership tracks store traffic recovery.

Execution now centers on boosting store traffic and improving retail conversion during the peak winter buying season.

Use Rocket Money if you have more subscriptions and provider bills than you’ll cancel or negotiate yourself, since its paid plans do that work for you. With only a few bills, skip the monthly fee and make the cancellations and calls on your own.

Where was Nvidia’s security team?

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Outsiders had little reason to notice when Nvidia’s longtime chief security officer and head of product security stepped down in August. Just two and a half months later, CEO Jensen Huang himself left the company over growing questions about his extramarital relationships — starting with an executive at Megaspeed, a Singapore data center company that U.S. investigators suspect of funneling Nvidia chips to China, then widening to reports about two separate affairs with Nvidia employees.

At the time, David Reber, who spent four years as CSO and seven at Nvidia overall, said he was leaving for health reasons. “This wasn’t a pivot I had planned. But I know what needs to come first: family and health,” he wrote on LinkedIn in late August, disclosing that he’d been diagnosed with young-onset Parkinson’s more than two years ago.

Reber’s replacement, Saša Zdjelar, previously spent 17 years at ExxonMobil in Houston, leaving as a senior IT manager before spending one year at Salesforce and several years at the cybersecurity company ReversingLabs. Zdjelar didn’t respond to questions Thursday about the transition or what the security team knew.

Nothing public connects Reber’s or Zdjelar’s role changes to the Megaspeed matter, but it seems fair to wonder what Nvidia’s security team knew about the government’s scrutiny of Megaspeed and about Huang’s relationships, and whether anyone raised concerns. Reber didn’t respond to questions Thursday. Nvidia also has not answered our related questions.

Not all of those relationships are the business of the security team. Relationships with subordinates typically fall to HR, legal, and the board. But an affair with an executive at a company under federal investigation is a different animal. (Megaspeed has denied any wrongdoing.) Security specialists say affairs can create vulnerabilities that foreign intelligence services look to exploit.

Catching that kind of exposure isn’t easy. The way intelligence services collect information, much of it through everyday conversation rather than hacking, makes it especially difficult for security teams to identify risks, according to retired senior intelligence officer Nicholas Eftimiades, who says he has trained more than 1,000 corporate security professionals.

Some people inside Nvidia clearly knew parts of the picture. Nvidia’s top lawyer was engaged with the government’s Megaspeed inquiry more than a year ago, The New York Times reported earlier this week. (Nvidia has said it regularly contacts officials.) Reports about Huang’s relationships with employees suggest colleagues also knew of them, even if the behavior is not expressly forbidden by Nvidia’s code of conduct. The Times cited five Nvidia employees who said he was involved with a vice president who reported directly to him, and Reuters, citing two people, later reported a second relationship. What’s unclear is whether any of that reached security.

Even if it had reached Nvidia’s security team, Eftimiades, who wrote Chinese Espionage Operations and Tactics in 2020, said a security team’s resources are often limited. For one thing, they’re often underfunded because, unlike some other cost centers, they don’t drive revenue. It’s astonishingly common for CEOs to answer a security team’s warnings with “not a nickel more than compliance,” he said.

Many companies also define security very narrowly, specialists say. In an opinion piece in The Hill, Don Aviv, CEO of the corporate investigations firm Interfor International, and author Jeremy Hurewitz, wrote, “Too many companies still approach security primarily as a matter of protecting facilities, preventing cyberattacks, and responding to physical threats.” The two argued that “[s]ecurity teams need to pay greater attention to insider threats, including the cultivation of executives and employees by foreign intelligence services, suspicious business relationships, conflicts of interest and attempts to obtain sensitive information through trusted personal contacts.”

Still, some of it is beyond what any team can do. Speaking generally, not about Huang, Eftimiades offered a hypothetical in which a CEO complains that he can’t do something because “I’m fighting a board who doesn’t want to do this.” That tells an intelligence asset who the obstacle is. The service then asks, “Who’s the sticking point here?” and looks for ways into that person’s email and phone.

What a rival intelligence service wants is often less technology than policy insight, including who’s pushing to change export rules and who the biggest blocker is. “Intel world tends to be like cancer,” he said. “Once it gets into the body, it looks to spread and looks to reach out.”

The playbook isn’t new, Eftimiades added, “particularly for China.” Any executive dealing with the country “will tell you that they’ve been approached.” He said there are also “tons of diplomat stories” of U.S. diplomats walking into a hotel room to find a woman on the bed. He said what’s newer compared with Soviet era spying is the relationships these services cultivate now often last years, not weeks.

Technology can augment that human intelligence, Eftimiades said, pointing to IMSI catchers are one example. These devices mimic a cell tower so that nearby phones connect to them. A basic version can cost about $1,000 and fit in a suitcase, he said, though government-grade models cost far more.

Eftimiades says no one knows yet if these were used against Huang, whose phone was presumably hardened such that compromises could be detected, if not stopped altogether. But either way, what Nvidia’s security team knew, and whether it could have acted on it, should become clearer as the board’s independent investigation and federal inquiries proceed.

In the meantime, Eftimiades said he hopes the whole episode is “a wake-up call” for U.S. industry, which he said is mostly “clueless” about China’s tactics. “We have lost trillions [of dollars] in intellectual property,” is his guess. “Whatever we think we’re doing isn’t working.”

I Just Found Out Why Disneyland Doesn’t Do Fake Snow During Rainstorms (Because Eww)

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

If You’re Looking For A Christmas Movie Outside Of Netflix And Hallmark, Hulu Has You Covered This Week

Netflix’s A Dog’s Perfect Christmas Made Me Cry, But Not For The Reasons I Thought

Upcoming Hallmark Movies: Full List Of New Premiere Dates, Including The 2026 Countdown To Christmas

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Don't let it snow, let it snow, let it snow!

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

As a native Southern Californian, I’ve never seen a white Christmas. The closest I can get without getting on a plane is during Disneyland’s holiday season. Every night, without fail, Main Street U.S.A. and the area in front of “It’s A Small World” become a winter wonderland, and it feels like a fantasy. That is, unless it's raining. I didn’t know before exactly why rain would stop the snow from falling upon Disneyland, but someone just made the reason quite evident.

“Disneyland’s Wintertime Enchantment” makes snowfall in Southern California possible every night at 7:30 p.m. and 8:30 p.m. However, don’t count on it if it’s raining. And FYI, there’s a lot of it coming this week after some notable flooding at Disneyland last month. Here’s why:

A post shared by Molly Evans (@happilyevansafterall)

Now, I hate to ruin the magic, but Disneyland snow isn’t actually snow. It’s something that’s been named "snoap" by fans, because it’s practically soap disguised as snow. It's dispersed by hidden rooftop machines.

According to the Los Angeles Times, Disneyland’s snow is made up of 98% water mixed with a non-hazardous, proprietary surfactant (soap) blend. On any other day, the “snow” evaporates shortly after landing somewhere and leaves no trace on guests or around the park. However, when too much water mixes with it, you get a very soapy (and gross) ground!

As you can see in the Instagram post, when the “snoap” and rain happen at the same time, it creates a major slip hazard for the guests. Therefore, it is usually canceled on rainy days. In this recent instance, the guest reports that snow was accidentally turned on at 10:30 p.m., causing an issue everywhere in the park where snow fell.

Even if the snowfall is stalled by rain, most of the other holiday festivities can still happen during such weather conditions. The firework show will have to be canceled during active rain, heavy cloud cover or high winds, but the projections at “It’s A Small World” will typically still run.

The “It’s A Small World” ride also has a special holiday version if you’re not into that song. The World of Color - Season of Light over at California Adventure can also usually hold up unless rain is paired with severe wind or lightning. And you can meet Santa Claus at Mickey’s Toontown Trolley Barn, which is located indoors.

One of my personal favorite holiday traditions is the A Christmas Fantasy Parade. I just love seeing all the characters in their Christmas-themed outfits, too. Over at DCA, there’s also a holiday event called “Guardians of the Galaxy: Awesome Dance Off!” debuting at Avengers Campus. Disneyland’s holiday-themed entertainment runs until January 6 this year, so there’s plenty of time to enjoy this special time of year.

If you’re not able to make it to Disneyland right now, it’s also a good time to check out the 2026 Christmas movie schedule. A ton are available to stream with a Disney+ subscription!

Now she's into covering YA television and movies, and plenty of horror. Word webslinger. All her writing should be read in Sarah Connor’s Terminator 2 voice over.

‘Toy Story 5’ Producer Reflects On ‘Level Of Complexity’ In Technology That Film Franchise Has Grown To Match With Curly Hair Tool Written For Blaze – Contenders L.A.

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

As evidenced by the latest Toy Story film, technology is here to stay in toys and beyond.

For producer Lindsey Collins, whose credits also include Disney/Pixar‘s A Bug’s Life, Ratatouille, Finding Nemo and Turning Red, the key is balancing not overdeveloping the looks of animations in the films with moving them forward to suit advancements humanity has made and better capture certain details.

“[We] try to keep it so we’re not evolving it so fast or so much that people are like, ‘This doesn’t feel like the Toy Story I recognize or I know,’ but also make sure that we’re keeping it so that we’re taking advantage of all the things we can do,” Collins said on a panel at Deadline’s Contenders Film Los Angeles event. “The technology just lets us do anything now. At the time, it was so restricted, like no hair or fur on the dog, or you couldn’t have you have tight clothing on everybody. One of the things that we’re really proud of is the character Blaze, who we all love, who’s the friend that ultimately comes as the gift at the end of the film. Curly hair is really hard.

RELATED: ‘Toy Story 5’ Writer-Director Andrew Stanton Wants To Bring Nuance To The Conversation Between Kids & Technology Watch on Deadline

“We’ve done curly hair before,” she added. “We’ve done it on Brave, but it is a technology that’s really hard to make look right. So we wrote a whole tool for the first time on this film to really capture the beauty and the gorgeousness of Blaze’s hair, and it took a ton of consultants internally to make sure: ‘Does it feel right? Does that look right? Does that feel like it looks natural to her?’ Those are the kinds of things that we try to do to take advantage of all this technology now to go, ‘Oh my gosh, we can now actually start to deliver a real sense of texture and softness,’ and that’s the kind of stuff that we would have cheated on all the other films.”

Collins also marveled at the ability to create 50 Buzz Lightyears on 50 horses in the fifth film versus the possibility of rendering one in the 1999 original.

RELATED: The 65 Movies That Have Made More Than $1 Billion At The Global Box Office

“The level of complexity [of things] we can now do is pretty amazing,” she said.

There was a version of the film’s ending where star character Jessie (voiced by Joan Cusack) meets an older version of her previous owner, Emily.

“Because it was Jessie’s story, we knew we wanted her to reckon with the past with Emily, and there was a version at one point where she actually meets Emily, all grown up and old, and and we were like, “That’s a trip. OK, does that feel satisfying?’ As an audience, there was a lot of conversation around what’s going to be the satisfying gift for Jesse at the end,” Collins said. “Is it seeing Emily? Is it kind of understanding, getting the chance to kind of play with her again? These toys are like vampires; they don’t age. The rest of the world ages around them. We knew it had to be something that felt like it was the gift for her that she needed, the closure, and so that last scene with the buried treasure under the tree was the thing that probably took the longest to land.”

RELATED: The 30 Highest-Grossing Animated Films Of All Time At The Global Box Office

Collins closed the panel with reluctance that the films could eventually center AI, but she also didn’t rule out a next film to complete the “Bonnie years” trilogy just as Andy got three films.

Bilt Will Stop Awarding You 4% Bilt Cash Earning At Card Renewal—Unless You Change This App Setting

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

I receive compensation for content and many links on this blog. Be aware that websites may earn compensation when a customer clicks on a link, when an application is approved, or when an account is opened. Citibank is an advertising partner of this site, as is American Express, Chase, Capital One, and others. Any opinions expressed in this post are my own, and have not been reviewed, approved, or endorsed by my advertising partners. I do not write about all offers that are available -- instead focusing on miles, points, and cash back (and currencies that can be converted into the same). Terms apply to the offers and benefits listed on this page.

Bilt Rewards is changing how rewards are earned on their credit cards. Today, cardmembers have a choice:

Earn 4% back in Bilt Cash, in addition to points. That Bilt Cash can be redeemed for points-earning on housing payments, faster points-earning (accelerators) and redemptions on hotel stays or with partners like Blade, Lyft, Walgreens, etc.

Skip Bilt Cash and earn ‘up to 1.25x on housing payments’ based on your month’s spending. Bilt is moving everyone over to the second option, however existing cardmembers and those who apply, are approved and accept their card through December 31 can opt in to preserving #1. It’s possible to do better with earning up to 1.25x on housing payments, but for most people doing significant spend on the card (where they’re earning more Bilt Cash than their housing payment would consume) they’ll come out ahead earning Bilt Cash. And of course you can always choose to move over to the new model later if you prefer! You need to proactively choose continuing to earn Bilt Cash, to avoid moving over. Once you actually move over (starting at your card renewal date) it cannot be reversed. I’ve already protected my Bilt Palladium Card (See rates and fees) with the legacy earning structure. This is the card I use for most of my spending. Here’s where you go in the app to do it. Go to your wallet and select your Bilt card. Then either go to your current choice for Bonus Rewards (e.g. “Flexible Bilt Cash”) underneath Your card rewards or go to Manage card and select bonus rewards which is the last option at the bottom. Select ‘your rewards are updating’ which is the top option on the screen. The default, which is already selected, is the new rewards model. Swipe right… Select ‘flexible Bilt Cash’ and hit save. You have until your card renewal date to make this choice, but if you preserve the old earning model you can always make the (irreversible) switch later. I’ve already made my choice. Florida city cancels St. Patrick's Day parade, citing new anti-DEI law

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Florida Gov. Ron DeSantis (shown here in October) signed the anti-DEI law that Delray Beach officials say prohibits them from putting on cultural celebrations, like a St. Patrick's Day parade. Alex Wong/Getty Images hide caption

One of the first casualties of a new Florida law that prohibits taxpayer dollars from going toward diversity initiatives is a St. Patrick's Day parade in a beach town that has been celebrating its Irish American community for nearly 60 years.

The city of Delray Beach announced this week that it was canceling its popular St. Patrick's Day festivities in compliance with a new law that takes effect on Jan. 1. The city will also withdraw its sponsorship of LGBTQ Pride celebrations in June, officials said.

The law, backed by Republican Florida Gov. Ron DeSantis is part of the state's ongoing crackdown on diversity, equity and inclusion (DEI) initiatives that the governor has called discriminatory and said have "no place" in public institutions.

The city lawyer for Delray Beach, a community in southeast Florida with picturesque beaches and a historic downtown, told the mayor that any government-sponsored celebrations of culture, ethnicity or heritage that are not a national holiday (for example: Independence Day) could run afoul of the new anti-DEI law. So, Mayor Tom Carney said, he made the difficult decision to cancel one of the city's biggest events of the year.

In past years, the parade, an annual event since 1970, wound its way through the downtown core of Delray Beach with spectators lined up several-people deep along the route. Local businesses and organizations sponsored floats and giveaways. School bands played as they marched through the streets. Participants included members of the Ancient Order of Hibernians, the largest and oldest Irish Catholic organization in the country.

"I've said it's engrained in the DNA of Delray and I do mean that. It's one of our signature events," Carney, a Republican, told NPR.

The mayor said he's hopeful that the city can find a path forward — and a way to preserve the event.

If a private sponsor were to volunteer to take over the St. Patrick's Day parade, or the June Pride celebrations, Carney said, the city would gladly hand over the reins and support the continuation of these festivities.

"These types of events we should be celebrating because in this country, people can have these events. In other countries, you can't do this. We have great freedoms here," he said. "And if the community wants something and it's not against public policy then I would support them to do it if they're using private dollars. If we can do it, we should do it."

Carney, who is of Irish descent, said the parade was privately run until about eight years ago — so returning to a private-sponsorship model would be a callback to the parade's origins. His own family, he said, was involved in keeping the parade going during the 1980s and 1990s.

Delray Beach officials said they have been inundated with emails, calls and social media messages from constituents who are angry that the community's decades-long celebration had been axed.

"It was kind of a call to action to me," Carney said. "The community really wants to have it, so we should go ahead and have it, we just need to get it privately funded."

The governor, meanwhile, has accused Delray Beach of weaponizing the new anti-DEI measure to make a political point. DeSantis said the city was "engaging in "malicious compliance" in a message he posted on X following the city's cancellation announcement.

"What they are doing is not mandated by Florida law," the governor wrote. "It's just an attempt to make a political point — a point that legacy media will no doubt try to amplify. We've seen these stunts throughout the years. Rinse and repeat."

The mayor said the city is merely trying to stay out of trouble with the law. The measure, SB1134, asserts that any official that violates the provision could face civil or legal consequences.

"The legislation has unintended consequences. The governor may genuinely believe what he believes and I take him at his word; our city attorney came up with a different conclusion," Carney said. "I'm going to leave it at that."

NPR does not offer or accept money for coverage or interviews. Anyone who makes such an offer or request is not an NPR employee and does not represent NPR.

Netflix's New Firefly Replacement Series Can Be Binged In Less Than 4 Hours

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

While it has been a long time since Firefly graced the airwaves, Netflix’s new space western is a four-hour binge that offers an ideal alternative to the iconic series. There are few one-season TV shows that have quite as long a half life as the short-lived space western Firefly. Firefly’s 14-episode run was met with mixed reviews and underwhelming ratings, but that wasn’t the end of the space opera’s story.

Firefly’s superb DVD sales, as well as the outspoken fan community online, prompted a movie spinoff in the form of 2005’s Serenity. Even though Serenity wasn’t a box office hit, the movie was beloved by critics, and a massive array of subsequent sci-fi shows acknowledged Firefly as a formative influence. There is SyFy’s space opera Killjoys, which often feels like the show Firefly could have been, as well as 2015’s Dark Matter, which was a love letter to Firefly in tone and premise alike.

In 2016, Jonathan Nolan’s Westworld reboot took Firefly’s mix of a futuristic sci-fi setting and traditional western elements and turned these same ingredients into a darker, more austere dystopian mystery box series. More recently, Netflix’s latest high-profile show has blended western and sci-fi tropes in a fresh, original story that has more than a touch of Firefly to it. An animated show created by LeSean Thomas, Bass X Machina takes place in a steampunk wild west where supernatural threats, giant robots, and gunslinging cowboys vie for dominance.

Within this pitiless milieu, Brian Tyree Henry voices the titular Bass Reeves, a bounty hunter and Civil War veteran. Joining Bass is Tati Gabrille’s Dana, a teenage engineer searching for her uncle who has disappeared, and Cree Summer’s Ahni, a magical mystic. With a superb ensemble voice cast including Wayne Knight and Janelle Monáe, Bass X Machina has no shortage of star power.

However, much like Apple TV’s Murderbot, this sci-fi series isn’t reliant on its impressive cast alone to keep viewers engaged. With epic, immersive battles, constant twists, and a wild mix of genres at its core, Bass X Machina is a truly fresh take on the real-life story of Bass Reeves that is unpredictable, inventive, and brimming with the unlikely fusion of sci-fi and western plot elements that made Firefly so memorable all those years ago.

On the big screen, attempts to mix sci-fi and westerns have often fallen flat. One need only look at expensive misfires like 2011’s Cowboys & Aliens and 1999’s Wild Wild West to know that, for whatever reason, the pairing doesn’t seem to attract interest from audiences at the box office. Moreover, even the kindest critics would struggle to call these movies overlooked classics.

Westerns and sci-fi don’t seem to mix at a practical level when it comes to feature films, at least not on a blockbuster scale. In contrast, Bass X Machina proves that Netflix’s animated series could take the same character who was the subject of a po-faced Taylor Sheridan Western series, Lawman: Bass Reeves, and have him fighting killer machines alongside paranormal mystics in a twisted reinvention of the Wild West.

With stunning imagery that calls to mind Netflix’s earlier genre blender Blue Eye Samurai, Bass X Machina admittedly makes a pretty dramatic visual contrast with Firefly’s deliberately low-tech vision of the far future. However, fans of Firefly’s fast-paced action, sharp dialogue, themes of found family, and truly original mix of the two genres will find plenty to love here.

Only time will tell if Bass X Machina will receive a second season, or if the show will suffer the same ignominious fate as Firefly as well as sharing its atypical mix of genres. However, for now, Netflix’s Bass X Machina is an ideal substitute for any Firefly fans who still miss the influential show.

Paul Bettany Reveals “Questions” About Bringing Wanda Back For ‘VisionQuest’ But Decided Show Should “Stand On Its Own”

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Food  |  Read Source →

While Elizabeth Olsen‘s Wanda Maximoff makes a brief return in VisionQuest‘s credits scene, there were talks about bringing her back for a bigger presence.

Paul Bettany, who reprised his role as Vision and executive produced the MCU series on Disney+, noted that he and Kevin Feige ultimately agreed the show was worth letting it “stand on its own” as opposed to distracting audiences with another big character.

“There were questions about it, whether that should happen, whether it opened up too much for the MCU, whether they wanted to do it later. I don’t know,” Bettany told The Playlist. “But I remember having this conversation with [Feige], and he said, ‘You and Terry have done such a great job at making me care about Vision. I really don’t think you should distract from that by giving the audience that at the end of the [show]. It will just fundamentally all become about WandaVision again, and this should and can stand on its own.’ And I think he was right.” Watch on Deadline

Although Wanda doesn’t reunite with Vision or their sons in the series, the post-credits scene showed what appeared to be Doctor Doom reviving her from the rubble of Wundagore following the events of Doctor Strange in the Multiverse of Madness (2022).

With the scene appearing to hint at her presence in Avengers: Doomsday, Bettany admitted that he “would love” to reunite with Olsen for another series in their continuity, following WandaVision (2021), Agatha All Along (2024) and VisionQuest.

“Gosh, I would love for that to be. I don’t know,” he said. “I mean, at the moment, it doesn’t feel that way, but I’ve died so many times in this show, so you never actually know.”

Bettany continued, “And my experience of Hollywood tells me if something’s a big enough hit, people want to continue with that. So I mean, I guess we’ll see. And whether Vision continues at all is sadly a decision that is made further up the food chain than I sit.”

After Vision was reunited with both their sons Tommy (Ruaridh Mollica) and Billy (Joe Locke) in the VisionQuest finale, it’s possible we’ll see a family reunion with Wanda in Avengers: Doomsday, premiering Dec. 18 in theaters.

Olivia Wilde Shares Rare Insight Into Coparenting With Jason Sudeikis

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Olivia Wilde shared rare comments on coparenting kids Otis, 12, and Daisy, 10, with ex Jason Sudeikis six years after their breakup: "We have reached this healthy equilibrium."

Olivia Wilde is extending an invite into her family dynamic.

Six years after the Don’t Worry Darling star’s breakup with Jason Sudeikis, she shared rare comments about their coparenting relationship as they raise their kids Otis, 12, and Daisy, 10.

“I really take pride in how good we are at coparenting our kids in my family,” Wilde shared on the Nov. 14 episode of the New York Times’ The Interview podcast. “We have reached this healthy equilibrium.”

Yet, she admitted that achieving this dynamic can be difficult.

“It’s so hard,” the Invite director continued. “Anyone who pretends it’s easy—like, ‘Oh, you just consciously uncouple, and then you find a way’—that’s bulls--t.”

And Wilde emphasized that she and Sudeikis make a point to always be honest with their kids.

“I feel relieved that I’m able to give them a life with parents where there’s a real sense of authenticity in terms of how everyone’s feeling and doing,” she explained. “What happens is when you’re trying to hide [interpersonal conflict] from your kids, they sense it. They’re the most intuitive people in the house.”

“People start to feel this sense of like, ‘What is real? ‘Cause I can tell that something's being performed for me, but I can sense it's not real,’” the 42-year-old added. “And that I wouldn’t wish on anyone.”

And though Wilde and Sudeikis—who called off their engagement in 2020 after nearly 10 years together—didn’t work out as a couple, the House alum stressed that it was ultimately the best decision for their kids.

“It feels like a failure when you can’t make a relationship work,” Wilde admitted, “particularly if it’s with the person you have children with.”

The I Want Your Sex star added that the breakup was “by far the most difficult, painful decision I’ve ever made in my life—to understand that it was not better for my kids to live in a house where their parents were not aligned.”

But despite Wilde and Sudeikis’ busy careers, the former pair always make showing up for their children their priority.

"I’m really grateful for the opportunity to have this space with my kids when I am completely with my kids, completely engaged with them,” Wilde shared during a June appearance on the Call Her Daddy podcast. “I really value the opportunity to be completely present with them and then to hand them off to a very capable coparent who is very present with them when he’s with them and then I have this space which is permission to go work my f--king ass off.”

For more celebrity exes who have remained on good terms, keep reading…

No, they are never, ever, ever getting back together. XRP to $1.3? Kalshi Traders Make Weak November Bets

📅 2026-11-15  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

XRP has continued to fall to levels not seen in the last three months as prolonged market volatility continues to intensify, causing the asset to post bigger daily price losses.

While momentum appears to be fading rapidly, the slow price movements have caused expectations for a November recovery to weaken, especially among prediction market traders.

Latest data from leading prediction market Kalshi shows that XRP traders have little confidence that the asset can still reclaim $1.30 before the end of the month.

While the asset saw higher expectations from Kalshi traders last month, it appears that its recent price dip below $1 has lowered their expectations as traders are assigning low probabilities to XRP reaching higher price targets this month.

Following their predictions, XRP currently has 7% odds of trading above $1.40, while the probability of reaching $1.50 stands at 8%.

More notably, bets on XRP surging to around $1.60 are even weaker, with only a 4% implied probability, suggesting that traders have increasingly lost confidence in XRP’s potential to achieve a major recovery before this month runs out.

The weak XRP predictions from Kalshi traders have come as XRP struggles to maintain momentum around its recent price levels, falling substantially below its local highs.

As of the time of writing, XRP is trading around $0.9892, representing a substantial decline of about 3.94% over the past 24 hours and an 11.3% drop over the last week.

It is important to note that the Kalshi data only reflects traders' positioning amid current market conditions and therefore does not guarantee XRP’s precise price movement in the future.

While XRP has continued to struggle below the major $1 level, it would need to stage a major recovery to reclaim the $1.30 level.

Calciomercato no stop - Indiscrezioni, trattative e retroscena del 14 novembre

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

La Juventus guarda anche a Gustavo Varela per rinforzare il proprio reparto offensivo. L'attaccante portoghese del Monza, attuale capocannoniere della Serie A con 9 reti, è finito nel mirino dei bianconeri, alla ricerca di un centravanti da mettere a disposizione di Luciano Spalletti già nel mercato di gennaio. Come riferisce il Corriere dello Sport, Varela rappresenta una possibile alternativa qualora la dirigenza juventina non riuscisse a concretizzare un'operazione per un attaccante di maggiore esperienza. Il suo rendimento ha attirato l'attenzione di numerose società, rendendo però particolarmente complicata un'eventuale trattativa. Si tratta di Napoli in Serie A, ma anche club esteri come Barcellona, Borussia Dortmund e Lipsia.

Como attento ai talenti che si mettono in mostra in giro per il mondo. Uno di questi è Hamza Abdelkarim, punta classe 2008 di nazionalità egiziana, legato al Barcellona da un contratto valido fino al 30 giugno 2030. Il giocatore starebbe valutando di lasciare il club blaugrana dopo aver faticato a ritagliarsi uno spazio in prima squadra (appena 12 minuti tra tutte le competizioni) sotto la guida di Hansi Flick. Cesc Fabregas, al contrario, potrebbe garantirgli un impiego superiore con due soli attaccanti in rosa (Douvikas e Kean), mentre dal canto suo Abdelkarim non sarebbe così propenso a rimanere nella cantera del Barça a 18 anni.

Il PSV Eindhoven insiste per Kevin De Bruyne, ma il Napoli non ha intenzione, almeno per il momento, di privarsi del centrocampista belga. Il club olandese vorrebbe provare a convincere l'ex Manchester City a trasferirsi già a gennaio, anche se l'operazione si presenta complicata. Come riferisce il Corriere dello Sport, la posizione della società azzurra è chiara: De Bruyne resta un elemento importante per Massimiliano Allegri, che vorrebbe continuare ad averlo a disposizione nella seconda parte della stagione.

"Possono pagarti quanto vogliono, ma lo stress è tuo, del tuo corpo”. Parlava così Stanislav Lobotka prima dell'ultimo infortunio muscolare che lo ha fatto fermare per sei settimane. Possibile un suo rientro intorno a metà dicembre, ma nel frattempo il Napoli deve fare di necessità virtù, utilizzando Gilmour come schermo davanti alla difesa. Non è la stessa cosa, ovviamente. Lo slovacco percepiva 2,5 milioni di euro all'inizio della sua avventura a Napoli, mentre nel 2024 ha contrattato uno stipendio variabile verso l'alto fino a 4,5 milioni. Una cifra che per il club di De Laurentiis incomincia a essere troppo alta, al netto del Decreto Crescita: Lobotka difficilmente sarà una plusvalenza ed è possibile che vada via a parametro zero, nell'ottica di rifondare e cambiare la filosofia contrattuale. Sulla falsariga di quello che succederà ad Anguissa, insomma, la sensazione è che sia l'ultima stagione per Lobotka a Napoli.

Attenzione al futuro di Hakan Calhanoglu. Il centrocampista turco è a scadenza di contratto con l'Inter e come normale che sia la sua situazione inizia ad attrarre gli occhi indiscreti di chi lo prenderebbe volentieri con sé a parametro zero. In particolare negli ultimi giorni si è discusso dell'interesse della Juventus, oltre che di quello del Como. Al momento però c'è un ma importante dato che il calciatore, almeno fino a questo momento, dà priorità all'Inter. Il club avrebbe poi promesso al giocatore e ai suoi agenti di voler parlare di rinnovo attraverso una formula con più bonus e uno stipendio fisso un po' più basso. Bisogna però poi trovare gli accordi poiché Calhanoglu ha la testa all'Inter e i dirigenti gli hanno promesso di sedersi a un tavolo per trovare una soluzione. Si vedrà dunque come proseguirà la situazione.

Luis Henrique è tornato ad allenarsi in gruppo e sarà a disposizione di Cristian Chivu per la trasferta contro l'Atalanta. Il recupero dall'infortunio, tuttavia, non sembra destinato a cambiare le gerarchie sulla fascia nerazzurra. Secondo Tuttosport, l'ex Marsiglia rischia di restare ai margini anche nelle prossime settimane, complice la crescita dei concorrenti. Djed Spence si è ormai imposto sulla destra, mentre Andy Diouf rappresenta un'alternativa preziosa anche a gara in corso. Sull'altra corsia, Federico Dimarco sta ritrovando continuità e rendimento, con Carlos Augusto che ha sfruttato le opportunità ricevute, dimostrando di poter incidere sia da esterno sia da braccetto. Una concorrenza che ha progressivamente ridotto gli spazi per il brasiliano. Un impiego limitato che potrebbe spingere il giocatore a valutare nuove opportunità già nella finestra invernale. In estate la Roma aveva mostrato interesse, mentre in precedenza il nome dell'esterno era finito anche sul taccuino di Tiago Pinto per il Bournemouth. Il brasiliano, tuttavia, non aveva manifestato particolare entusiasmo per la destinazione giallorossa e aveva respinto gli approcci dalla Turchia, preferendo attendere eventuali proposte dalla Premier League. Lo scarso minutaggio a Milano potrebbe ora portarlo a rivedere le proprie priorità.

La Roma lavora per rinforzare la rosa in vista della sessione di mercato e cerca quel trequartista sinistro di piede destro che Gian Piero Gasperini chiede fin dal suo arrivo nella Capitale. Tra i diversi tavoli aperti dal ds Tony D'Amico, uno dei principali porta a Londra, sponda Chelsea, per Jamie Gittens. Il Chelsea potrebbe aprire a una cessione in prestito con diritto o obbligo di riscatto, sebbene a Stamford Bridge credano ancora nelle sue qualità mostrate in passato con il Borussia Dortmund. La Roma valuta attentamente le condizioni fisiche del giocatore, chiuso nella rosa inglese da profili come Neto, Estevao, Quenda e Rogers, ma resta fortemente interessata a riportarlo al centro del progetto tecnico di Gasperini per rinforzare la corsia offensiva sinistra nella seconda parte della stagione calcistica.

In casa Napoli, qualcosa potrebbe accadere per gli esterni visto che Lang è in uscita (anche se per il momento non sono arrivate offerte) ma qualche dubbio c’è anche su Neres, su cui c’è molto interesse in Brasile. E allora ecco che in questo caso ci sarebbe modo di inserire un giocatore con quel tipo di caratteristiche. Il profilo che piace di più è sicuramente Cacciamani già cercato anche dalla Roma in estate. Calvin and Hobbes’ 5 Greatest Spaceman Spiff Comic Strips

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Send this article to your favorite space crustacean

Send this article to your favorite space crustacean

Calvin and Hobbes wasn't Bill Watterson's first pitch for a comic strip. C&H's protagonists began as side characters in Watterson's comic The Doghouse before his original syndicate suggested that they ought to be the focus. "Marvin" became "Calvin," the rest of The Doghouse's cast was dropped, and while United Feature Syndicate still didn't take the resulting comic, Universal Press Syndicate did.

Before then, Watterson had drawn cartoons about a spacefaring character as early as college. Watterson went on to make that character the focus of one of his rejected comics: a mustachioed, cigar-smoking parody of adventurers like Flash Gordon. Shortly after Calvin and Hobbes began, Watterson reworked "Mort" into Calvin's fantasy alter ego Spaceman Spiff.

Over the course of Calvin and Hobbes' run, Spiff was one of Calvin's most frequent escapist characters. Whenever Calvin was caught in a boring or unpleasant situation, he frequently retreated into his Spiff persona to pass time or even make a hasty retreat. In practice, that gave Watterson a handy excuse to take Calvin and Hobbes' art well outside its usual mundane setting, with tons of gross aliens, impossible landscapes, and great phrases like "eat hot electric death" that don't usually come up in everyday conversation.

Much like the rest of Calvin and Hobbes, Spaceman Spiff really hit his stride in the comic's last three years, when Watterson began doing his trademark "unbreakable" Sunday strips. Some of the most colorful and evocative C&H comics are Spaceman Spiff adventures from the mid-'90s, but Spiff made a few solid appearances before that point. Here are some of our picks for the best Spaceman Spiff comics in Calvin and Hobbes.

When Calvin gets sick during his summer vacation, his first reaction is to get annoyed. Sick days are for the school year. I think we all can relate.

Unfortunately for all involved, Calvin's mom takes him to the doctor. That includes a checkup, an injection, and a retreat into Calvin's Spiff mode, as he imagines himself as a prisoner of aliens rather than a little kid trapped in an examination room.

Calvin's teacher Mrs. Wormwood catches a lot of strays in Spaceman Spiff cartoons. Sometimes I want to see a version of C&H from her perspective. Most of her class are apparently boring normal kids who do the reading and don't get in unusual amounts of trouble. Then there's Calvin, who's as much of a chaos agent as a six-year-old can be. Mrs. Wormwood and Mr. Wilson from Dennis the Menace would have a lot to talk about.

In this early installation, Calvin gets called on in class and seamlessly incorporates Mrs. Wormwood's history question into his daydream. It's not being called on in class; it's being interrogated by the Hideous Zorgs of Planet X-13. (Look at those bug-men. Those are aliens that deserve the word "Hideous" being part of their proper noun.)

The most extreme (in the '90s sense) Spaceman Spiff adventure was a week-long story where Calvin got deeply enough into character that he staged a breakout from elementary school. Called to the board to answer a math problem, Calvin instead decides to shoot his way out (with a rubber band), then run for the door.

Through what one assumes is the relative indifference of school staff, Calvin manages to make it all the way home before his mom catches him. Unfortunately, Spiff can't get Calvin out of the ensuing consequences.

Right from the start, Bill Watterson has said that one of his primary influences on Calvin & Hobbes is George Herriman's Golden Age newspaper comic Krazy Kat, which ran from 1913 to 1944. That was already some inside baseball in the 1980s when Calvin and Hobbes was new, as Krazy Kat’s modern following is almost exclusively made up of historians, fanatics, and other cartoonists. It’s not dead media yet, though, as historians and preservationists have made a point of keeping collections of Krazy Kat in print. Curious readers can check out Fantagraphics' George Herriman Library hardcover collections, which are still in print at time of writing.

If you check out Krazy Kat, its influence on Calvin and Hobbes is obvious immediately, particularly in its colored Sunday comics. Herriman's characters inhabited a cartoonish version of the Arizona desert, and many of Watterson's Spaceman Spiff strips feature him visiting alien planets with similar arid backgrounds. This largely-wordless strip from 1993 is a perfect example, with so much of it devoted to Calvin's imagined spaceship journey from an alien valley to home.

One of the best Spaceman Spiff strips comes in the last year of Calvin and Hobbes. Without dialogue, we see the entire comic from Calvin's perspective, as he's entertaining himself during yet another boring class by imagining himself behind the stick of Spiff's flying saucer.

Suddenly, Mrs. Wormwood interrupts his daydream and breaks his train of thought. As soon as she isn't paying attention anymore, Calvin switches to a new scenario: this time, he's a dinosaur stalking prey.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

The best of Polygon.com, directly in your inbox.

Netflix Officially Brings Back a Hit 3-Part Rom-Com Series

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Netflix has built a strong foundation of romance titles over the years. 2026 alone saw the return of some of their heaviest hitters, with Bridgerton returning for Season 4 towards the beginning of the year and Heartstopper officially wrapping up with the release of its feature-length finale, Heartstopper Forever. The teen drama My Life With the Walter Boys also welcomed viewers back to Silver Falls with Season 3, with plenty of swoon-worthy moments to boot. When it comes to rom-com series specifically, though, there's only one that everybody wants more of.

Netflix has officially renewed its red-hot story of star-crossed lovers for another run as of November 6. It's part of a surprising run of renewals for the streamer this month, alongside Adults, Youth, Outlast, and, in a history-maker for the platform, Virgin River, which will become its longest-running scripted drama by a number of seasons at nine. Nobody Wants This continuing is no shocker, given the viewership and Season 3 rating as its second-best on Rotten Tomatoes with a Certified Fresh score of 83% from critics. Erin Foster, Jenni Konner, and Bruce Eric Kaplan will be back as co-showrunners, writing, "We are so happy and thrilled to do another season of our favorite show on television, which coincidentally is the one we run."

Four love stories, four completely different ideas of what it means to be somebody's person. This is the one that matches how you actually fall, and what it would ask of you.

You do not fall for strangers. You fall for the person you have already been talking to for years, which is the slowest and by some distance the most durable way anybody has found to do this.

You want a partner, not an audience. Somebody who wants their own life as badly as you want yours, and who would never ask you to be smaller to make the relationship easier.

The intensity was never the problem. The secrecy is. You are drawn to a love that lives entirely in private, and to the particular ache of wanting somebody you are not supposed to want.

You do not do this cautiously. When it happens for you it is immediate and total and it rearranges everything, and you would not accept a smaller version of it.

Season 3 ended with wedding bells ringing for Noah and Joanne on the beach in a surprise ceremony in front of no one but God and a random man walking his dog nearby. What was initially meant as a trip to finalize Joanne's conversion to Judaism turns into a sacred moment for the couple that binds them together forever. However, they still have an entire squabbling family to break the news to, all of whom are sure to be very normal about it.

There's also the reason for their bickering to deal with — Sasha is caught between his estranged wife Esther and Morgan, with both finding out about his hook-ups with the other. His story is expected to get a big spotlight in Season 4, with all of Stephanie Faracy, Michael Hitchcock, Paul Ben-Victor, Tovah Feldshuh, Andrew Rannells, Sarah Silverman, Shiloh Bearman, and Sherry Cola likely returning alongside the aforementioned stars.

History Says This Is the Best Investing Move You Can Make if a Bear Market Is Coming

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Should investors wait for stocks to fall before buying?

Right now, the U.S. stock market feels a little shaky. Inflation is still well above target. The Federal Reserve is on track to raise interest rates again next month. And the Iran war shows no signs of ending.

The S&P 500 (^GSPC -0.40%) is down a modest 3% in the last month, while the Nasdaq-100 is off nearly 10%.

All of this is to say that many investors are considering the possibility of a bear market right now. While earnings expectations for U.S. companies are still looking strong, there are enough concerns about the future of the artificial intelligence (AI) building boom, the biggest driver of recent economic growth, that investors are looking at ways to protect themselves.

And it's a good thing, too. The best time to think about your investment process is before something happens.

Market declines tend to trigger some of the worst investor behaviors. If stocks are declining, there's an inclination to stay away because 10% drawdowns often become 20% to 30%. It happened in 2000-2002, 2007-2009, and 2022.

But those declines also have a recent history of rebounding quickly, often before investors even noticed. These occurred in late 2018 and early 2025 during President Donald Trump's Liberation Day tariffs, and in the more extreme case of the COVID pandemic.

In other words, nobody knows whether a correction is the end or the beginning of a deeper drawdown. And even if you did know which one it was going to be, market timing requires you to be right twice: once when you decide to get out of the market and once when you later get back in. Plus, that requires a discipline many investors lack.

You can still take advantage of lower share prices during bear markets by simply maintaining your monthly investing schedule. If you have money going into a brokerage account every month or every paycheck into something like a 401(k), keep doing what you're doing.

This process allows you to buy the dips and lower your average cost without worrying about whether it's the right time to trade. Plus, bear markets often last far less time than the average bull market.

If you try to time the market, there's a good chance that the rebound will have already started once you decide it's OK to return to stocks. By automating, you may not get the absolute lowest prices on your purchases, but you'll certainly get at least some at discounted prices.

As always, investing is one of the few things where doing less can yield better results.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 823%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 209% for the S&P 500.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

She-Hulk's Tatiana Maslany Starred In A Must-Watch 2026 Apple TV Series

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Tatiana Maslany's career exploded in the mid-2010s with her virtuoso multi-character work on BBC America's brainy sci-fi thriller series "Orphan Black." It was a very good show, but the deeper Maslany got into her roles, the more you wondered what she could do in a film or series where her performance prowess could be concentrated on one specific part. Indeed, she proved to be unsettlingly fierce as the vengeful Sister Alice on HBO's dark reinvention of "Perry Mason" and brought all the charm she could muster to Marvel Studios' irreverent "She-Hulk: Attorney at Law." Her talent was undeniable, but the projects weren't proper showcases of her astonishing ability.

This changed in 2026 with the premiere of Apple TV's comedy-thriller series "Maximum Pleasure Guaranteed." The show stars Maslany as Paula Saunders, a fact checker (the noblest and most under-appreciated of journalistic professions) who's fighting for custody of her child while looking into the kidnapping and apparent death of a webcam model named Trevor (Brandon Flynn). No less an authority than Stephen King has effused that "Maximum Pleasure Guaranteed" is "like Hitchcock came back to do it one more time." Its first season is streaming in its entirety on Apple TV, and it's officially coming back for more. So, now is the time to get caught up before you're 20 episodes deep down a binge hole.

The tone-setting pilot of "Maximum Pleasure Guaranteed" was directed by David Gordon Green, who was once a Terrence Malick-ian stylist (see: early triumphs like "George Washington" and "All the Real Girls") before he segued to mainstream movies via broad stoner comedies like "Pineapple Express" and "Your Highness." But while "Maximum Pleasure Guaranteed" possesses a bit of visual sophistication, it's the writing by series creator David J. Rosen and his staff that puts it over the top.

The casting is also key. Jake Johnson is deliciously antagonistic as Paula's ex-husband Karl, who's seeking sole custody of their child while she nurses her anguish with wine and digs into the fate of her newfound internet companion. Rosen and the show's writers pile the complications on with inventive abandon, and Tatiana Maslany surprises every step of the way as a protagonist we simply can't figure out.

"Maximum Pleasure Guaranteed," as mentioned, has been renewed for a second season, so Maslany will get another 10 episodes to explore possibly the best character she's ever been given to play. In the meantime, you should get out to see Noah Segan's "The Only Living Pickpocket in New York," where she is dynamite as John Turturro's daughter. The Maslany-ssiance is upon us.

There's a hidden way you can check which services are running on your Android phone

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Just like a desktop PC, your Android phone has a built-in task manager. Here is how to find it, monitor your memory and safely limit background apps.

If you use a desktop PC, you may be familiar with the task manager, which lets you view all the processes running in the background of your computer and taking up resources. What you may not know is that Android phones (even budget models) have the same feature, but it's kept hidden away from users, requiring a little digging to find it. If you want to keep an eye on what's using your phone's memory, it can be worth uncovering this hidden display, but you should proceed with caution before you start closing processes willy-nilly.

To check what processes are running, you first need to activate developer mode to make your phone faster. Under the Settings menu, scroll down to the bottom, tap About Phone and look for the Build number. If it's not there, you may have to enter an additional Software information menu, depending on your phone's model and Android version. Once you find it, keep tapping the Build number option until you get a message that developer mode has turned on or a dialog box asking you to confirm. Then, back out to the main Settings menu. You'll find Developer options on this main screen, or under a System submenu. Tap the option, then open Running Services. You can also select the three dots in the top right of the screen to select Show cached processes and see what's been recently suspended but still held in memory.

This is Android's version of the task manager, showing you all the running services currently providing frameworks for the apps on your phone, along with how much memory they're using. But don't start closing processes to free up that precious memory. While the task manager on PC often shows plenty of bloat that you can safely close, that's not usually the case for Android phones. The processes you see here are more likely managing features like Bluetooth, your keyboard and location settings. Shutting these down can seriously hamper your phone.

Both the Running Services and Memory menus are more like diagnostic tools. If you're having problems with your phone, it can be helpful to check here and see if anything seems wildly out of order, like a single process hogging the majority of your phone's memory. You may want to stop the related process until you can fix the underlying issue, but be prepared to restart your phone if anything goes wrong.

A better way of controlling background processes is by limiting their use. In the Developer options menu, under the Apps heading, you'll find Background process limit. This lets you limit the number of apps allowed to run in the background at one time. This default setting triggers an automatic limit based on how much RAM your phone has, which is a crucial factor in making older Android phones last longer. However, you can also change the limit to between one and four to free up memory.

Keep in mind that this will make multitasking harder and will cause a bigger performance hit at the moment you re-open apps, since your phone will have to fully restart them now.

You can also limit how individual apps behave in the background. In the Settings menu, head to Apps, select a specific app and tap Battery. The default Optimized option automatically adjusts the app's background permissions based on how you use it, but switching to Restricted limits its activity when suspended.

Cowboys vs 49ers: 2 players to buy, 2 to sell against San Francisco

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Dallas Cowboys are coming off a narrow win over the Indianapolis Colts, and now the 7-1 San Francisco 49ers are coming to town, bringing one of the league's most complete rosters and a defense that has consistently given Dallas problems in their recent matchups.

There are some opportunities for the Cowboys, particularly with CeeDee Lamb playing at a high level, but there are also some difficult matchups that could expose weaknesses on Dallas' roster. Here are two players to buy and two to sell ahead of Sunday's game.

I mean, who else would this list start with? Lamb has been the best player for the Cowboys all season, and there's no reason to think that Dallas will stop feeding him the ball. Lamb is coming off a monster performance against Indianapolis, hauling in 10 catches for 195 yards and a touchdown on 16 targets. It was his second-highest receiving total in a game during his career.

Through nine games, Lamb has 70 receptions for 904 yards and seven touchdowns. He's already surpassed the 900-yard mark, and his 108 targets lead the Cowboys by a wide margin. The matchup against San Francisco is difficult, but Lamb is the kind of receiver who can change a game regardless of the opponent. With George Pickens possibly missing another week and Dallas dealing with inconsistencies elsewhere the offense, Dak Prescott should continue looking Lamb's way early and often.

The 49ers will undoubtedly have a plan for him, but Lamb's recent production makes him difficult to leave out of the buyers market. He's been the Cowboys' most reliable playmaker overall, and Dallas needs to find ways to get him involved.

At some point, the Cowboys have to figure out how to beat San Francisco, and Dak Prescott has to be a big part of that solution. Unfortunately, his history against the 49ers doesn't inspire much confidence. Prescott is 2-4 against San Francisco in his career, including an 0-2 record in the playoffs. In those two postseason games, he has thrown for 460 yards, two touchdowns and three interceptions. His most recent regular-season meetings haven't been much better. He threw three interceptions in the 2023 blowout loss and two more in the 2024 matchup.

Across his four regular-season games against San Francisco, Prescott has completed 65.5% of his passes for 875 yards, eight touchdowns and five interceptions. That's not necessarily disastrous production, but the turnovers have been costly, particularly against a team that knows how to capitalize on mistakes.

The 49ers have won four straight meetings against Dallas, and Prescott will have to overcome both that team history and his own struggles against this opponent. San Francisco's defense is disciplined, physical and capable of making quarterbacks pay for forcing throws.

With Fred Warner leading the unit, Prescott will have to be sharp with his reads and decision-making, especially against the blitz, where he has looked terrible this year. Dallas needs a big performance from its quarterback to pull off the upset. Given the history, though, this is a matchup where I'm selling Prescott until he proves otherwise.

With Jake Ferguson ruled out due to a knee injury, Brevyn Spann-Ford has an opportunity to take on an even bigger role in the Cowboys' offense. It might not be the most obvious matchup to exploit, but he could be a sneaky contributor against San Francisco.

Spann-Ford has caught 15 passes for 130 yards this season, and his role has been growing. Against Indianapolis, he played 55 offensive snaps compared to Ferguson's 27, catching two of three targets for 20 yards. That workload suggests the Cowboys are already comfortable giving him significant playing time.

The 49ers have plenty of defensive talent to account for, and their attention will naturally gravitate toward Lamb and the Cowboys' other primary playmakers. That could leave opportunities for Spann-Ford underneath or in the middle of the field, particularly if Dallas uses him to help with protection before releasing him into a route.

This isn't a prediction that Spann-Ford is suddenly going to become the focal point of the passing game. Rather, it's a bet that his increased playing time and the attention paid to Dallas' other weapons could create a few favorable opportunities. With Ferguson unavailable, the Cowboys need someone else to contribute from the tight end position. Spann-Ford is in a good position to do that.

The Cowboys have indicated that Jaishawn Barham could see an increased workload as injuries continue to pile up on the defensive side of the ball. That's understandable from a personnel standpoint, but it also creates a potential problem against an offense as versatile as San Francisco's.

Barham had some encouraging moments early in the season, but his limitations have become more apparent as the competition has increased. His playing time has also been limited recently, which makes the prospect of a larger role against the 49ers particularly concerning.

San Francisco is exactly the type of opponent that can put a young linebacker in difficult situations. Kyle Shanahan's offense uses motion, misdirection and mismatches to create space, and open up running lanes.

If Dallas gives him more responsibility, San Francisco will have plenty of opportunities to test him. The Cowboys need all the help they can get defensively, but this is a tough week to ask an inconsistent young player to take on a bigger role.
