Could everything be broken? Grounded jets, Microsoft outage, AI chaos make some wonder

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

"There's a lot of weird stuff going on," Chuck Amasio, a 68-year-old from Rahway, New Jersey, said. "It makes one think."

Maybe the trouble started with the AI billionaire and the Silicon Valley chaos that followed talk of his infidelity, including with an alleged Chinese spy. Or maybe it was the Microsoft outage that, depending on how you looked at it, either cast millions of frustrated workers adrift or spared them from a daily, unrelenting electronic avalanche.

But by Friday afternoon, when U.S. aviation authorities ordered the grounding of planes across the country, it felt to many that something was up. Something was broken.

Just ask Chuck Amasio, a 68-year-old from Rahway, New Jersey, trying to fly out of Newark Liberty International Airport when his canceled flight led to a few beers with his brother in an airport bar and eventually a night in a hotel. Saturday morning, he was back at the airport to try again.

“There’s a lot of weird stuff going on,” Amasio said. “It makes one think.”

His first thought was dark: “We talk about how wars aren’t done with bombs anymore. They shut down airspace or a power grid. They can cripple an economy.”

Throw in a few more examples just from Friday â a cruise ship that overturned in New Orleans when a tanker slammed into it, suspicious drones over a U.S. air base in Britain, a bomb found near an ethanol plant in Ohio â and perhaps things seem even weirder, opening the door for a writer who has been picking at America’s fears for generations to offer his own explanation.

“The aliens have landed,” Stephen King wrote in a Friday afternoon tweet. “That’s the REAL reason for the ground stop. They don’t want to start a worldwide panic.”

The official explanations for the thousands of grounded jetliners and hours of email and other computer troubles were far more prosaic.

A technology upgrade on the nation’s air traffic control system had overloaded antiquated equipment and caused “intermittent” communications and radar gaps, leading to the ground stop that lasted more than four hours, administration officials said.

By Saturday morning, air travel was already smoother and Bryan Bedford, administrator of the Federal Aviation Administration, said airline schedules should be back to normal by late Sunday.

Microsoft, for its part, said Saturday that its services had been fully restored, saying the problem “was introduced by an issue during an earlier network capacity upgrade that surfaced” following a recent network change.

The outage is believed to have affected many millions of people around the world.

Maxine Gustafson, a 34-year-old physician assistant from Minneapolis, was in line to pick up a hastily booked rental car at Chicago’s O’Hare airport on Friday afternoon so she and colleagues could get home from a work training.

Gustafson said the week’s mishaps made her feel afraid and helpless.

“It makes you think, like, is this a cyberattack?” she said. “We don’t have control over a lot of this stuff.”

Both the FAA and Microsoft said there were no signs that malicious activity had been behind the troubles.

Tori Perkins, 34, was hoping for a Saturday flight at Terminal 2 at San Diego International Airport, where 11 of the 62 flights already were listed as canceled that morning and the same number were delayed. Crowds were normal.

“It’s one of those things where I don’t necessarily believe what the government says,” said Perkins, who seemed to take the setback in stride. “I don’t know what’s true because there are so many people saying different things.”

Perkins, who lives in Hawaii, was in San Diego on a layover on her way to see family in Florida. She spent Friday night at the airport.

She was not surprised that Secretary of Transportation Sean Duffy had made a plea Friday for tens of billions in more funding to upgrade the country’s notoriously outdated air traffic system.

“It sounds like more of the same,” she said.

The FAA has already replaced thousands of copper wire connections with fiber optic lines and upgraded hundreds of radio systems as part of the overhaul that began last year after Congress approved $12.5 billion in funding. The White House has been pushing for $30 billion more to complete the work.

Sometimes chaos brings out the best in people.

Corbyn Wascher was stuck on the tarmac at Charlotte Douglas International Airport for four hours while heading back to Moline, Illinois, from Miami, where he had been at an education conference.

He said everyone was cordial on the plane, talking to one another and stretching their legs. He was impressed with the American Airlines staff, who handed out water, pretzels and biscotti snacks, though they were clearly under stress.

“The stewardesses were amazing,” said the 37-year-old high school teacher.

Back at the Newark airport, Caroline Ruiz had caught a few hours of sleep by a heating vent. She wasn’t sure what time it was as she waited for someone to bring her a meal she was promised after the cancellation of the first leg of her 24-hour trip to Australia. She hoped to get started later Saturday.

It was two days in a row, she noted, where she had been betrayed by technology.

Though maybe one of those days didn’t count as a betrayal.

“I kind of got a free day yesterday” because the Microsoft systems were down.By TIM SULLIVAN Associated Press

Cape Verde election could deliver first woman president since independence in 1975

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Polls closed Sunday evening in the vote in which former Justice Minister Joana Rosa is challenging President JosÃ© Maria Neves, who is seeking a second term.

PRAIA, Cape Verde (AP) â Cape Verde residents voted Sunday in a presidential election that could make Joana Rosa the country’s first woman head of state since independence from Portugal in 1975.

Polls closed Sunday evening in the vote in which the former justice minister is challenging President JosÃ© Maria Neves, who is seeking a second term. Six candidates are competing. If none wins more than half the votes, a runoff will be held Nov. 29.

Rosa has the backing of the opposition Movement for Democracy, which governed for a decade before losing May’s parliamentary election. Women have held senior Cabinet positions, but the Atlantic island nation off West Africa has never had a woman president or prime minister.

The archipelagic country in the Atlantic Ocean, off the coast of West Africa, is battling with challenges such as a high unemployment rate and cost of living.

“I see many promises that are not fulfilled. I am tired, but I am still voting,” Aderito Cardoso, a 44-year-old resident of Villa Nova in the capital city of Praia, said after casting her vote.

Under Cape Verde’s parliamentary system, the prime minister directs policy and holds most executive power. The directly elected president’s role is largely ceremonial, although the office carries the power to veto legislation.

The country of about half a million people has a record of competitive elections and peaceful transfers of power that stands out in the region. Nearly 77,000 citizens living abroad are registered to vote, accounting for almost a fifth of the electorate.

Roselma Ãvora, a professor of political science and gender studies at the University of Cabo Verde, said limited economic opportunities were pushing young people to leave. Many voters also feel disconnected from political leaders and want a president who understands their daily struggles, she said.

Neves, who won the presidency in 2021 with about 52% of the vote, has promised to build cross-party support to improve housing, healthcare and education, create jobs and raise wages.

His incumbency has given him greater visibility, including among overseas voters, Ãvora said. Rosa’s candidacy, meanwhile, challenges traditional expectations of women in a society that remains “very patriarchal.”

Rosa has focused her campaign on the balance of power, arguing that the presidency should serve as a counterweight to the African Party for the Independence of Cape Verde, which backs Neves and controls the government and most municipalities.

The party, known as PAICV, won a parliamentary majority in May, ending a decade of rule by the Movement for Democracy. A Rosa victory would again place the presidency and government in opposing political camps.

For Cape Verde-based writer RosÃ¡rio Luz, party loyalties matter more than Rosa’s gender.

“We have a lot of women in politics, but we don’t have the policies for women that we should have,” she said.

Women shoulder much of the financial responsibility for their households, but government policies have failed to address that burden, Luz said.

Megaspeed says Alice Huang left in 2025 as company conducts internal probe over Nvidia fallout

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A customer and cloud partner of Nvidia, Megaspeed has faced scrutiny in recent months, including from U.S. authorities, over allegations that it circumvented U.S. export restrictions to route large quantities of advanced chips to Chinese companies.

Singapore-based cloud computing company Megaspeed International said Sunday that Chinese businesswoman Alice Huang, whose relationship with former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang sparked U.S. national security concerns, left the company last year.Megaspeed, which is under scrutiny over alleged transfers of advanced Nvidia chips to Chinese companies, also announced an internal investigation led by outside lawyers.

The company told The Associated Press that it runs a “legitimate and lawful business” and was aware of media reports containing claims about its business, supply chain and global operations “that are not supported by verifiable evidence.” It did not address specific allegations.

“The company has retained external legal counsel to conduct a thorough internal investigation,” it said in an emailed statement.

Alice Huang “left Megaspeed effective Aug. 30, 2025, and currently has no role with the company,” the statement said.

“Megaspeed does not comment on Ms. Huang’s personal and private matters.”

A customer and cloud partner of Nvidia, Megaspeed has faced scrutiny in recent months, including from U.S. authorities, over allegations that it circumvented U.S. export restrictions to route large quantities of advanced chips to Chinese companies. Last December, it said there was “no evidence” of illicit chip transfers.

Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent said Sunday that “it doesn’t matter to me” whether Alice Huang is a spy. He said his concern is “whether Nvidia had a culture of compliance around the U.S. export restrictions” or not.

“Jensen’s foibles are his foibles. Obviously, it’s a problem if in fact he was having relationships with subordinates,” Bessent said in an appearance on Fox News Channel’s “Sunday Morning Futures.” “I want to keep coming back to: ‘What was the culture at Nvidia, and were they pressing these seemingly illicit sales to goose their revenues and their earnings?’”

Bessent said the Nvidia board of directors should look outside the company for a new CEO because “they need a dramatic shake-up.”

Singapore’s government said Thursday that it initiated an investigation into Megaspeed in February 2025, including possible “false declarations in relation to the purchase of servers.”

Authorities also investigated Alice Huang, whom the government identified as Megaspeed’s then-CEO, chairperson and one of its key controllers.

Singapore authorities questioned her and withheld her passport last year. They later concluded there was insufficient evidence that she had committed a crime, despite suspecting she had provided “inaccurate” information to downplay her role at the company.

An earlier New York Times report suggested U.S. officials believed Alice Huang was a Chinese spy recruited to potentially influence Nvidia to make its advanced chips more available to China.

As scrutiny intensified and reports of her romantic relationship with Jensen Huang gained attention, Nvidia announced Thursday that he was stepping down as CEO.

U.S. Attorney General Todd Blanche said Thursday, however, that the Justice Department had not concluded whether Alice Huang was a Chinese spy. Asked whether she was a spy, President Donald Trump said: “I don’t know if that’s been determined yet.”

Beijing rejected the allegations as unsubstantiated.

The Associated Press has been unable to reach Alice Huang for comment.

Megaspeed began as the overseas arm of a Chinese video game developer and was incorporated in Singapore in 2015 as 7Road International. It adopted its current name in March 2023, when all its shares were transferred to Huang Le Limited, a British Virgin Islands company. Alice Huang is also known by her Chinese name, Huang Le.

By November 2023, she was no longer listed as a Megaspeed director, and all her shares had been transferred to an entity owned by Singaporean businessman Tan Yong Pong, also known as James Tan.

Despite those public records, short seller Culper Research cited a Megaspeed employee in a report released this May as saying Alice Huang was “actually still our boss” and remained involved in daily operations.

The company’s finances have also attracted attention. Singapore corporate records show its total assets rose roughly 90-fold from end-2023 to 2024, reaching almost $3 billion.

In a separate statement Thursday issued under James Tan’s name, Megaspeed said it takes its obligations under U.S. export control laws “extremely seriously.”

The company said it had cooperated with inspections by the U.S. Commerce Department’s Bureau of Industry and Security and was ready to cooperate fully with other relevant U.S. authorities.

2 planes collide on the ground in Miami, only minor injuries reported

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The accident involved a Copa Airlines Boeing 737 aircraft with 172 people aboard and a UPS Boeing 767 cargo plane.

MIAMI (AP) â Federal aviation safety officials on Sunday were investigating a minor collision between two planes at a taxiway intersection at Miami International Airport, where one aircraft’s wingtip struck the nose of another, authorities said. Two people reported minor injuries.

The accident happened at about 9 p.m. Saturday and involved a Copa Airlines Boeing 737 aircraft with 172 people aboard and a UPS Boeing 767 cargo plane, according to the National Transportation Safety Board and Copa Airlines. The wing of the Copa flight and the nose of the UPS plane collided, the NTSB said.

Photos taken at the scene show the UPS plane nose with a horizontal gash on one side and a larger hole in the middle.

On audio recorded by ATC.com, a pilot on the UPS plane told air traffic control that he went too far at the intersection and his plane was clipped by the Copa flight. Air traffic control told the UPS pilot that he was supposed to yield to the Copa aircraft.

The NTSB said Sunday that preliminary information showed the UPS plane was instructed to give way to the Copa Airlines flight but did not stop. The UPS aircraft had substantial damage, while an assessment of the Copa plane had not been completed, it said. The NTSB said it opened an investigation and expected a preliminary report to be released within 30 days.

The Copa plane had just arrived from Panama City and the UPS plane was headed to Bogota, Colombia, officials said.

In a statement on social media, Copa called the collision minor and said two of its passengers were treated at the scene for minor injuries.

UPS said it was working with authorities to determine what happened.

“We cannot speculate on the details of the incident,” UPS said in a statement. “The National Transportation Safety Board is responsible for investigating incidents like this and will control the flow of information.”

The mishap came two months after an Amazon cargo plane careened off a runway at Miami International and smashed into a van carrying workers for a company that cleans airplanes, killing five people.

Central African Republic vs Benin - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Nsidibe Akpan | 14 Nov 2026 23:59

Central African Republic host Benin at the Japoma Stadium in Douala, Cameroon, on Monday in the fourth round of Group F in 2027 Africa Cup of Nations qualifying.

Benin won the first leg 4-1 in Abidjan on Thursday and lead Group F on goal difference, level on seven points with Burkina Faso, while Central African Republic remain bottom with no points and a goal difference of -6.

Central African Republic, known as Les Fauves, have lost all three qualifiers, scoring two goals and conceding eight, and have not won any match since October last year.

Mauritania won 3-1 in their opener on September 24, and Burkina Faso followed with a 1-0 victory over them on September 28.

Head coach Abdelaziz Kerkache, a Moroccan who has previously worked at Mouloudia d’Oujda, was appointed on November 6 to replace Eloge Enza Yamissi, only six days before the first leg.

Kerkache recalled Geoffrey Kondogbia of Olympique de Marseille and Le Mans forward Louis Mafouta, who both missed the opening two qualifiers, and Mafouta responded by equalising in the first leg.

Les Fauves had more of the ball in Abidjan, with 56% possession and eight corners to Benin’s five, yet goalkeeper Dominique Youfeigane faced eight shots on target and conceded four.

Central African Republic are still chasing a first Africa Cup of Nations finals appearance after narrowly missing out in 2021 and 2023, and their home leg is being staged in Douala rather than in Bangui.

Benin, nicknamed Les Guepards, have taken seven points from three Group F games under Gernot Rohr, beginning with a 1-1 draw at Burkina Faso on September 25.

A 2-1 win over Mauritania in Abidjan on September 29 followed, with Rodolfo Aloko scoring either side of half time, as Benin again used an Ivory Coast venue because the Stade de l’Amitie in Cotonou is not yet approved for qualifiers.

Hassane Imourane opened the scoring in the seventh minute of the first leg, Louis Mafouta equalised in the 19th, and Benin were 3-1 up at half time after Aloko struck in the 31st minute and Aiyegun Tosin in the 36th.

Tosin completed his brace in the 56th minute, and he and Aloko have now scored six of Benin’s seven goals in qualifying.

Benin won despite having only 44% of the possession, turning 18 shots into four goals, with eight efforts on target against Central African Republic’s 14 shots and five on target.

Rohr said afterwards that the scoreline flattered his side because Central African Republic attacked a great deal, and he expects a reaction in a rematch only four days later.

For the Central African Republic, Edwin Quarshie, booked in the 1-0 defeat by Burkina Faso, missed the first leg through suspension for accumulated yellow cards and should be available again on Monday.

Venuste Baboula, who has an ankle sprain, was left out of the starting lineup on Thursday, and he could be saved for the return match.

Kondogbia, Mafouta and Goduine Koyalipou all started in Abidjan, and Kerkache has had only one match to work with that group.

For Benin, captain and striker Steve Mounie remains out injured, so Rohr again leans on Aloko, Tosin and Junior Olaitan, who has three goals and seven assists for Besiktas this season.

Parfait Faton, a 16-year-old midfielder from AAS Sarcelles, received his first senior call-up in a 25-player squad that also includes Sessi D’Almeida and Jodel Dossou.

Benin were far more efficient than their numbers suggest, putting eight of 18 shots on target with only 44% of the ball, and they did it without Mounie.

Central African Republic have conceded eight goals in three qualifiers, and a second game in four days under Kerkache is unlikely to repair a defence that let in three before half time on Thursday.

Benin cannot afford to slip either, because Burkina Faso are also on seven points after an added-time winner from Elohim Kabore against Mauritania, and the Cheetahs lead the table only on goal difference.

A clean sheet is less certain, because Mafouta equalised in the first leg and Kondogbia gives Les Fauves a better platform than they had in September, so we expect Central African Republic to score but not to take anything from the game.

Germany vs Netherlands - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Anthony Nolan | 14 Nov 2026 23:59

Fighting for a place in the UEFA Nations League quarter-finals, Germany are set to host Group A2 leaders the Netherlands at Olympiastadion Berlin on Monday.

Die Mannschaft know that they likely need to win to progress, while Oranje through barring an unprecedented swing in goal-difference.

Jurgen Klopp's Germany are pushing to qualify for the Nations League quarter-finals for just the second time, but they face a tough test to reach the last eight.

Die Mannschaft are currently third in Group A2, level on eight points with Greece and three behind the Netherlands, and their chance to go through leans on the fact UEFA prioritises head-to-head points between tied teams.

Klopp's side were beaten 1-0 by the Azure and White on September 27 before drawing 0-0 with them on matchday four.

However, Ivan Jovanovic's men were hammered 7-0 by the Dutch on Friday, meaning that a German victory over the Netherlands on Monday would see them progress ahead of Greece regardless of their result against Serbia.

Die Mannschaft were held to a 1-1 stalemate by Oranje on matchday one, with Cody Gakpo equalising in second-half stoppage time to deny his former Liverpool manager maximum points.

However, Klopp's team have remained undefeated in the group since their loss to Greece, most recently beating Serbia 3-1 on Friday, when they raced into a 2-0 lead within the first 14 minutes thanks to an own-goal from Srdjan Babic and a follow-up by Florian Wirtz.

Meanwhile, Xavi's Netherlands have yet to taste defeat in the Nations League this term, and are in a commanding position at the top of their group.

Top of the pile with 11 points, Oranje's 7-0 demolition of Greece last time out - following a 2-2 draw on October 1 - means the only possibility of them finishing below the top two is if there is a dramatic 14-goal swing in the Azure and White's favour.

Xavi is without talismanic centre-back Virgil van Dijk this month, but his team were still able to keep their first clean sheet in the competition on Friday, while Tijani Reijnders's first-half hat-trick set the tone for his flourishing attack.

However, it remains to be seen whether the Netherlands can repeat that feat in Germany, considering that they have won just one of their last six clashes with Die Mannschaft as the away side.

On the other hand, Oranje are unbeaten in their last seven matches on the road, last suffered a penalty shootout defeat against Spain in March 2025.

Germany have no new injury issues to contend with, though they are missing Bayern Munich stars Jamal Musiala and Serge Gnabry due to respective muscle problems.

In their absence, Klopp could continue with Brajan Gruda, Florian Wirtz and Derry Scherhant behind Arsenal man Kai Havertz up top.

As for the Netherlands, they were forced to take off Frenkie De Jong at half time against Greece after he suffered back pain, and he has now left the national squad to return to Barcelona.

Teun Koopmeiners looks set to step in and fill the gap alongside Joey Veerman at the base of Xavi's midfield, while Tijani Reijnders operates in the number 10 role.

Further forward, Donyell Malen has been a standout for Roma this season with 12 goals across all competitions, and he should lead the line on Monday while Cody Gakpo and Crysencio Summerville provide width.

Captain Virgil van Dijk has sat out this international break with complaints of physical discomfort, so expect to see Tottenham Hotspur's Micky van de Ven and Jan Paul van Hecker start at centre-back.

Ter Stegen; Treu, Thiaw, Tah, Mittelstadt; Kimmich, Pavlovic; Gruda, Wirtz, Scherhant; Havertz

Verbruggen; Dumfries, Van Hecke, Van de Ven, Timber; Veerman, Koopmeiners; Summerville, Reijnders, Gakpo; Malen

The maths of the table is complex, but Germany have a simple task on Monday - they will likely need to beat the Netherlands to progress.

Oranje have yet to lose in the Nations League this season, though with the stakes lowered, they could be caught off guard.

Contenders Hall Of Fame Honoree Peter Farrelly Talks ‘I Play Rocky’, ‘Dumb & Dumber’, The Hair Gel Bit In ‘There’s Something About Mary’ & More

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Prolific director, screenwriter and producer Peter Farrelly reflected on some of his most famous films Saturday at Deadline’s Contenders Film Los Angeles event.

Contenders Hall of Fame honoree Farrelly talked with Deadline’s Mike Fleming Jr about his most recent film I Play Rocky and the process of bringing it to the screen. Earlier, Farrelly had appeared on the film’s panel with star Anthony Ippolito, who plays Sylvester Stallone in the film, and talked about the uphill battle to making the crowd-pleasing underdog tale.

Farrelly revealed that it was his daughter’s ex-boyfriend who first read the script and told Farrelly it “was the best script he had ever read.” Farrelly agreed and pushed forward with the film, noting it was imperative that he had Stallone’s approval on the script, which came quickly.

He also shared his own “Rocky” moment: “The bravest thing I ever did in my life was decide to write when I was in my mid-20s,” he said.

RELATED: ‘I Play Rocky’s Peter Farrelly Talks Why The Making Of ‘Rocky’ Was The Next Real-Life Story He Wanted To Take A Swing At – Toronto Studio

Farrelly also talked about his years and years of getting turned down for scripts and having to push through the rejection and not give up. As for 1994’s Dumb & Dumber, “We wrote that script five years before it got made, and it was the exact same script,” he shared. “Every studio passed, every actor passed.” He acknowledged that he changed the title to A Power Tool Is Not a Toy and “everyone started reading it.” It was the eventual film’s star Jim Carrey who told him Dumb & Dumber is the title, “so we went back to that,” he said.

“You’ve got to believe,, that they’re wrong and you’re right,” he said regarding the rejections. “You know, year after year getting turned down, it’s hard. You just gotta stick out and hope that you’re right.”

RELATED: ‘I Play Rocky’ Review: Peter Farrelly’s Sensational Biopic Of The Making Of Stallone’s Classic Is Just As Inspiring As The Original Movie – Toronto Film Festival

One of the most memorable and hilarious scenes in Farrelly’s films was in There’s Something About Mary and Cameron Diaz’s “hair gel.” “I realized we were going to have to go to R [in that film] because we were right on the PG-13 line.

“There’s two things that the studio didn’t want was the zipper joke in the beginning and the hair gel thing of course, and we understood that, especially the hair gel.” But of course, the hair gel stayed in and the rest is history.

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

It appears quarterback Jacoby Brissett earned a good grade in his crash course on the Ravens offense.

Brissett joined the team a little over a week ago in a trade with the Cardinals and is on track to start against the Chargers on Monday night. In comments to reporters on Saturday, head coach Jesse Minter didn’t make it sound like Brissett will be limited to a small portion of the team’s playbook when he takes the field.

The Ravens head into Monday’s game in last place in the AFC North, so they’ll need Brissett to operate at a high level right out of the gate to stay in the thick of the playoff race heading into the back half of the season.

Luckily no one was injured in the ordeal.

On Nov. 11, a gunman reportedly opened fire at the inXile office building in Tustin, California, with XBOX now saying no one was injured in the incident. Police have since apprehended the suspect, but motives are still not clear.

As NBC Los Angeles reported, a gunman had opened fire at a “commercial complex in Tustin” earlier this week on Nov. 11. “Several bullets struck at least one building in the area,” which seems to be inXile’s offices. At the time the gunman was said to have fled the scene, but Aftermath confirmed today that they were in custody. The police responded to the incident immediately after reports came of an alleged drive-by shooting.

"Each one of these cards is this window into this incredible world. It's not just world building—it's worlds building."

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Netflix's Magic: The Gathering series was initially announced in 2019 with the MCU's Russo Brothers at the helm, though they left a couple of years later thanks to the all-purpose "creative differences". That was all we heard until 2024 until Brandon Routh, who had been cast as planeswalker Gideon Jura, casually mentioned in an unrelated interview that the project had been binned.

Netflix announced soon after that actually the animated series was a go, now with Terry Matalas of Star Trek: Picard and the 12 Monkeys TV series as its head showrunner, with a teaser image showing pyromancer Chandra and leonin Ajani Goldmane silhouetted against a red sky.

Now, a mere seven years after the first reveal, there's a "sneak peek" trailer with a bunch of excited Wizards of the Coast staff, art labeled "early development", and a few seconds of Ajani and Chandra doing their stuff.

The most intriguing part is when, over a montage of 3D props including a car that looks like an art deco Batmobile from New Capenna, executive producer and supervising director Patrick Osborne says, "All the planes exist in their own pocket universe. That comes with culture, design language, architecture, and we're even making the film-making style different depending on where it is."

That's a good idea. An animated series with different art styles for each plane the characters travel to would be a great way of representing Magic, a card game where every artist gets to put their own spin on how things look. "Each one of these cards is this window into this incredible world," Matalas says in the trailer. "It's not just world building—it's worlds building."

As for the plot, Netflix's release sums it up as Chandra and a party of planeswalkers trying to prevent a war from consuming the entire multiverse. So a nicely low-stakes story then. It's scheduled for release in 2027. There's a live-action Magic: The Gathering movie still apparently in the works as well.

Declan Rice delivers verdict on £100m Arsenal transfer target after ‘contact made’

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Your weekly briefing on Arsenal, featuring team news and expert analysis from Matt Verri

I would like to be emailed about offers, event and updates from Evening Standard. Read our privacy notice.

Declan Rice insists that he is relishing the competition from Alex Scott with England, as Arsenal are linked with a potential big-money transfer swoop for the Bournemouth star.

Scott continues to go from strength to strength for both club and country, earning his first senior caps for the Three Lions during the previous international break in recognition for his fine form in the Premier League.

The 23-year-old made his England debut off the bench in September’s 2-0 away win over the Czech Republic in the Nations League, with Rice having pulled out of the squad, before then being handed his maiden start by Thomas Tuchel in the 7-0 demolition of Croatia last month.

Scott picked up a thigh injury during that camp that it was feared could keep him out for up to two months, though the issue was not as serious as first feared and he was back on the Bournemouth bench in their last game against Ipswich.

While Scott has been unable to press his claims for further England involvement during the current international window, Rice knows that he will be quickly back challenging for a regular starting role in Tuchel’s engine room.

“To be honest, Alex Scott playing was incredible,” Rice said of Scott ahead of Sunday night’s crunch clash with world champions Spain in Madrid, per The Mirror. “The way he's been playing for Bournemouth the last two or three seasons has been remarkable.

“The way he's being spoken about is totally right because of the levels he's set for himself and I totally understand playing for England comes with competition.

“When we [Arsenal] signed Bruno Guimaraes, when we signed [Martin] Zubimendi, when we had Myles [Lewis-Skelly], [Mikel] Merino, [Martin] Odegaard, you're competing with six midfielders at your club every day so for me there's nothing different.

“Throughout my whole career there's been competition. It's normal.”

Rice could yet line up with Scott at club level, with reports last week claiming that Arsenal have joined Chelsea and Liverpool in the race for his services ahead of the January transfer window.

The i Paper claimed that all three clubs had made initial contact with Bournemouth over possible moves for the former Bristol City star, with Chelsea seeing a number of summer bids rejected for a player who has turned down multiple offers from his current employers to extend a contract that is due to expire in June 2028.

Young talent will be trusted, Bruno Fernandes can thrive and touchline presence is vital - what Man United fans can learn as incoming boss Mauricio Pochettino masterminds 6-0 win for USA

📅 2026-11-14  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Published: 18:47 EST, 14 November 2026 | Updated: 19:37 EST, 14 November 2026

Mauricio Pochettino enjoyed a stellar penultimate game in charge of the United States, thrashing Haiti 6-0.

The newly appointed Manchester United manager has just one game left in charge of the USA before getting to work at Old Trafford.

He will link up with United on Wednesday, but first must navigate the second leg of their Concacaf Nations League quarter-final tie with Haiti on Tuesday.

Here, Pochettino cut a fairly reserved figure on the touchline as he was rewarded for trusting youth - and even saw his team score from a corner, something United don't do much of these days.

Without further ado, here are four things Manchester United fans learned from the win.

Mauricio Pochettino, dressed in a scarf, was fairly reserved as the United States beat Haiti 6-0

Pochettino’s arrival spells good news for United’s proud tradition of promoting homegrown youth - it is glad tidings for the likes of Shea Lacey and Kobbie Mainoo. You wonder if, for a while, JJ Gabriel will look on forlornly from Real Madrid’s reserves.

The Argentinian handed out 13 debuts in the September-October international break, including to eight teenagers in one 4-1 victory over Peru.

His strategy was no different here as he started 18-year-old Julian Hall up front and Diego Kochen, 20, in goal - both players he has given debuts to this year. He was rewarded, with Hall scoring his fourth goal in just five games for the United States, while Kochen kept a clean sheet.

Despite his affinity for the stars of tomorrow, he is a hard schoolmaster. In a column for The Athletic in 2022, he revealed that he sat a young Harry Kane down for a brutal meeting where he showed him stats and clips to justify why he wasn’t playing him more.

Pochettino had known Kane’s potential from their first training session but wanted the ‘tough conversation’ to get the best out of him. Kane went on to score 169 goals under him for Tottenham, and now he’s the best player in the world.

His trust in fresh faces could inject some much-needed enthusiasm around the club, strengthen the squad's connection with the fanbase, and increase the energy levels in one of the Premier League's oldest, most lethargic teams.

The likes of Shea Lacey will be delighted at how Pochettino puts trust in younger players

Julian Hall, 18, struck his fourth goal in five games for the United States under Pochettino

Pochettino has a history of getting the most out of attacking midfielders such as Dele Alli, Christian Eriksen, and Adam Lallana. You can add Cole Palmer to that list too, though he was primarily a right winger under him at Chelsea.

Manchester United desperately need him to get a tune out of Bruno Fernandes, who has cut a frustrated figure this season and whose creative efficiency has declined - though he still has nine goal involvements.

If Christian Pulisic’s performance against Haiti is anything to go by, Fernandes will be happy. The attacking midfielder scored twice, playing a free role and being given penalty duty.

The 28-year-old was also asked to muck in defensively, and that’s something Fernandes is willing to do. Across the seasons, he has often covered more ground than any of his team-mates.

Pochettino and Pulisic have seemingly had a distant relationship, despite the latter’s insistence to the contrary. The manager branded it ‘b*******’ when the AC Milan star opted out of the Gold Cup last year, and left him out of his selection a couple of months ago, bringing in 16-year-old Cavan Sullivan.

Asked about Pochettino's upcoming final game, Pulisic said at full-time: 'We're treating it like any other game.' No emotion on display.

Depending on how you interpret Fernandes - either a die-for-the-badge club servant or a primadonna - Pochettino could draw the best or worst out of the Portuguese.

Pochettino often gets the best out of attacking midfielders - will he work well with Bruno Fernandes?

He has had a distant relationship with Christian Pulisic, but the AC Milan star scored twice here

Pochettino tends to push his defensive lines high up the pitch and his strategy with the United States has been no different.

They were almost caught out for trying to play the offside trap early on as a simple ball from midfield sent Danley Jean Jacques through one-on-one, forcing Kochen into a good save.

Meanwhile, full-backs Antonee Robinson and Alex Freeman were constantly pushing high and overlapping in his 4-2-3-1 system - although you’d obviously expect a strong level of intent when they’re favourites against Haiti.

Given his high-line tradition, Pochettino may well favour more mobile defenders such as Leny Yoro and Patrick Dorgu.

The battle for Harry Maguire will be to convince his new boss that his other qualities compensate for his lack of pace.

Pochettino takes inspiration from the frenetic football of Marcelo Bielsa, who managed him at Newell’s Old Boys in Argentina.

But he'll have to balance his philosophy with the reality of United's woeful defensive record this campaign. Just the 15 goals conceded in the last five games...

Pochettino showed an eagerness to intervene early in the game - a possible boost after Michael Carrick suffered from slow starts

Pochettino is typically seen as an animated character on the touchline, in contrast to Carrick's statesmanlike behaviour, but he spent lots of time in his seat here.

He has calmed down somewhat these days - though he did kick a crate of bottles in frustration at the World Cup - and didn’t even react much when his team scored.

A major criticism of Carrick is that he takes too long to change things in-game, often going late with substitutions. Pochettino has traditionally leaned that way, too, but he was eager to influence proceedings here.

Even three minutes in, at a break while Antonee Robinson was being treated, he called Pulisic over for what looked like an intense tactical chat.

United have been plagued by slow starts this season, so it was encouraging to see Pochettino so alert and full of ideas early into the game. He could have coasted, really, but wanted to eke out every advantage.

La Lazio supera 3-0 il Mgarr United nell'amichevole disputata a Formello e mister Gennaro Gattuso traccia un bilancio positivo del lavoro settimanale dei suoi: "Abbiamo fatto due giorni ad alta intensità, oggi siamo soddisfatti". Il tecnico della capolista, intervenuto nel post partita ai canali ufficiali dei biancocelesti, ha sottolineato soprattutto l'importanza della mentalità della sua squadra: "Il modulo cambia poco, quello che cambia è la mentalità e la voglia di far fatica". Positivo infine il giudizio sul rientrante Albert Gudmundsson, definito un giocatore di qualità, seppure non ancora al massimo della condizione.

Sul mercato di gennaio, la priorità della Lazio sarà innanzitutto trattenere tutti i big, tra cui Kenneth Taylor, Nuno Tavares e Gustav Isaksen. In entrata, lo svincolato Mauro Icardi resta al momento soltanto un'idea per rinforzare il reparto avanzato, ma piace anche Dennis Man del PSV, già protagonista in passato in Serie A con il Parma. Un eventuale affondo dipenderà tuttavia dal futuro di Cancellieri: in caso di partenza dell'italiano, la Lazio potrebbe infatti valutare un'operazione in prestito con diritto di riscatto per il rumeno.

Ahead of ‘The Mummy 4,’ Brendan Fraser’s WWII Movie Is Officially Being Pulled From Streaming

He has covered everything from Marvel to the Oscars, and Marvel at the Oscars. He also writes obsessively about the box office, charting the many hits and misses that are released weekly, and how their commercial performance shapes public perception. In his time at Collider, he has also helped drive diversity by writing stories about the multiple Indian film industries, with a goal of introducing audiences to a whole new world of cinema.

Later this month, Netflix will release its hotly anticipated new WWII movie, The Mosquito Bowl, after giving it an unusually long theatrical run. The Mosquito Bowl was released in approximately 350 theaters domestically on October 30, marking the streamer's second film this year to get a proper theatrical window, after the Spanish-language title La Bola Negra. Netflix has gone back on its stance about theaters, and has slated several major movies for a theatrical bow in the coming months. Also this year, the streamer will debut the Once Upon a Time in Hollywood spin-off, The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth, exclusively in IMAX.

The streamer's new WWII movie is currently slotted for a November 25 release on the service. It concludes a particularly packed year for WWII content, both on the big screen and small. One of the year's runaway home-video hits happens to be a WWII thriller that received tremendous reviews during its run; the movie in question is currently streaming on Peacock in the United States, but only for a little while longer.

It debuted theatrically in May, at a time when audiences were lapping up last year's holdover WWII hit Nuremberg. The new film grossed more than $15 million in its domestic run, overtaking not only Nuremberg but also the year's first Jason Statham vehicle, Shelter, and the Alan Ritchson action movie Runner. Since its release, fans of WWII storytelling have also been offered the 20-episode History Channel documentary series World War II with Tom Hanks and the action movie Lucky Strike.

Four icons and four completely different ways of walking into trouble. This is the one you would have been out there, why it fits, and what it would have cost you.

You have never explained yourself and you have never needed to, because people work out fairly quickly that you are the most capable person in the room without you ever having to mention it.

You are the funniest person in most rooms and the most loyal person in all of them, and you would be genuinely insulted if somebody suggested those two things were in any tension.

You never wanted this job, and you took it anyway because the alternative was standing there watching somebody less careful do it badly, which is the most Western reason there has ever been.

Nobody has ever successfully talked you out of something you had already decided was right, and a long line of grown adults has found that out the hard way.

We're talking, of course, about Pressure. Directed by Anthony Maras, the film stars Brendan Fraser and Andrew Scott in the lead roles, and follows the real-life story of a British meteorologist who convinced U.S. President Dwight D. Eisenhower to push the storming of Normandy by one day. The film received excellent reviews and is now sitting at a "Certified Fresh" 90% critics' score on Rotten Tomatoes. Its "Verified Hot" 95% audience score on the aggregator website played a key role in its home-video success.

The site's consensus reads, "Finding a fresh angle on one of the most dramatized days in military history, Pressure is a brainy war film that derives most of its thrills from Andrew Scott's simmering performance." According to FlixPatrol, Pressure has spent more than 50 days on the domestic Prime Video chart and nearly 25 days on the domestic Peacock chart this year. However, audiences keen to check it out on the former don't have much time, as it'll be removed from the service on December 1. Stay tuned to Collider for more updates.

Danny Boyle Says Tabloid Headlines Censored In British Version Of ‘Ink’ Will Be Shown In U.S.

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Ink director Danny Boyle on Saturday confirmed that redacted Sun newspaper headlines shown as part of an epilogue in his Netflix drama about the founding of the UK tabloid will be shown in full in the U.S. theatrical and streaming version of the film.

Speaking while receiving the Deadline Legend Awards during Deadline’s Contenders Film Los Angeles awards-season event Saturday, Boyle said an edited version showing the headlines is currently being edited into a new cut of the film in time for its December 11 release in select theaters.

The pic, based on James Graham’s play and adapted screenplay that tells the story of Rupert Murdoch and editor Larry Lamb’s takeover of the British tabloid The Sun in the late 1960s, setting the tone for new kind of journalism in the process, was unable to show the headlines owing to Britain’s stringent libel laws.

“We’ve had a battle to get the film to where it is now, and I can tell you — I can say this, can’t I? — I think that we just won the ability [to show the headlines] because we’re in America, and you have better protection for freedom of speech than we have in Britain,” Boyle said during the Q&A session at the DGA Theater, adding that he hopes the uncensored version “will be [in] the finished version of the film, which we’ve never been able to show in Britain.” Watch on Deadline

Boyle pointed to one specific headline in the epilogue that was redacted in UK versions of the pic, above an article about the 1989 death of 97 fans crushed while trying to enter Hillsborough Stadium to watch a Liverpool-Nottingham Forest FA Cup soccer game. The tabloid report blamed Liverpool fans for the tragedy, citing what turned out to fabricated police and official reports.

“There are various headlines … at the end of [Ink], and there’s one particular, which although it won’t mean anything to you, to anybody from Britain it does mean a great deal, which is that The Sun ran a terrible headline called ‘The Truth,’ which is one of the principal concerns of James Graham’s screenplay throughout the whole film,” Boyle said.“[The Sun] ran this headline, and it was a bunch of lies about the way that Liverpool fans were killed at the Hillsborough disaster,” he continued. “It’s one of the low points of British journalism, and we wanted that to be always in the film, and we weren’t able to have it in the British version. We have these redacted versions, but know that we’re in America. We’ve got it in there, and I hope that the tidal wave will then reach back to Britain, and we’ll be allowed to put them in there because it’s an important moment for freedom of speech, which you have enshrined.”

He added, “You know, it’s it’s it’s interesting that a tabloid that you know that lets it all hang out would try to stop you from doing something similar.”

The epilogue in the pic, which stars Jack O’Connell as Lamb and Guy Pearce as Murdoch, is also key as it ties The Sun’s rise to modern tabloids — and Murdoch’s eventual founding of Fox News.

“What they ignited … and the pact that they came up with to start The Sun culminates financially and principally in the birth of Fox News,” Boyle said. “Because the money that The Sun made allowed Murdoch to buy 20th Century Fox and then to finance the establishment, the extraordinary establishment, of a new terrestrial channel in America, which takes a lot of money. So there’s an umbilical cord, I think, that connects these two guys and their meeting in 1969 with where we are now.”

Ink, which has been hitting festival since its world premiere to open this year’s Venice Film Festival, is due to open in the UK on January 8, when it becomes available to stream on Netflix.

Common reasons your Roku TV can turn on by itself and how to fix it

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Some people have been experiencing random power-ons recently.

You're sound asleep in the middle of the night when, all of a sudden, your TV turns on. You know you turned off the TV before you went to bed, no one else is awake, and there's nothing near your remote to accidentally push the power button. What — or who — could have turned on the TV? A bored spirit haunting your home? A burglar looking to take a quick break?

Those scenarios are highly unlikely. But, unfortunately, some Roku TVs and Roku streaming device owners have been jolted out of their slumber by those products inexplicably powering on randomly over the past few months. And no one really knows why it's happening. There may be a few explanations, however, and a couple of potential fixes to try if your Roku device has gone rogue during the wee hours.

When Roku OS version 15.3 went live in August 2026, owners of Roku TVs and Roku streaming devices began reporting these middle-of-the-night disturbances. Not every Roku TV or streaming player has been impacted by this supposed 15.3 bug, but the timing of the reports with the software update seems more than coincidental.

What's odd about that, though, is that there was nothing in the 15.3 update that involved autoplay features or even power management. So, in theory, the changes that were made to the Roku OS shouldn't have spurred these instances of people's Roku TVs "waking up" without being powered on by anyone (or anything) and playing a TV show or movie. But we all know that software updates often introduce bugs with no real rhyme or reason, and that feels like the most logical explanation for these strange occurrences.

Having your Roku TV's HDMI-CEC feature enabled is another very plausible reason the TV powers on randomly. If you're not familiar with HDMI-CEC, it's basically a chain that links all your A/V components with built-in HDMI switching (like soundbars, gaming consoles, receivers and speakers) since they're all connected via HDMI cables. And this allows all of those devices to be controlled with one remote.

So, when you power on your Roku TV, for example, HDMI-CEC essentially notifies all the other connected components to turn on. All it takes is one glitchy signal to confuse the whole chain, which could lead to a random power-up. And there is a history of HDMI-CEC causing strange switching between inputs and components, as well as phantom power-ons. Samsung Anynet+, Sony BRAVIA Sync, LG SimpLink and Roku 1-Touch Play are all just different names for the same HDMI-CEC standard, but each has its own custom software added to it to create the proprietary HDMI-CEC feature for each brand of smart TV.

Unfortunately, compatibility sometimes becomes an issue unless all of your connected devices are made by the same company (though even that doesn't guarantee incident-free performance). This can cause signal miscommunication that results in the random behavior of your Roku TV or streaming device.

There are other possible explanations for your Roku TV turning on by itself. For example, the Fast TV Start feature is a proprietary power-management setting that keeps your Roku TV's OS on in the background even when you shut off the TV.

This allows the TV to turn on instantly without any boot time, and it keeps the TV on alert for voice commands to turn the TV back on. Fast TV Start has also seemingly caused phantom power-on problems in conjunction with the Roku OS 15.3 update, as the always-on network connection kept open the door for overnight glitches.

You may try all of these suggestions and still not have much luck because the root of the issue has not been "officially" identified by Roku, or anyone else, for that matter. And without the root cause, there remains no guaranteed fix. But these possible solutions are certainly worth trying and just might do the trick.

The first thing to try, if you haven't already, is updating your Roku TV's OS to version 16, which was released in October. Fixing the 15.3 bug wasn't part of the update, and no reports have surfaced that OS 16 patched the automatic power-on problem, but it's definitely worth trying. The worst-case scenario is that your Roku TV's OS is fully updated.

Next, disable 1-Touch Play (HDMI-CEC) not just on your Roku TV but on all the other components connected to the TV, regardless of brand. This includes your A/V equipment and gaming consoles. To do so on your Roku TV, go to Settings > System > Control other devices and uncheck 1-Touch Play. Also disable Fast TV Start, which you can find under Settings > System > Power > Fast TV Start, then either clear the Enable Fast TV Start option or check Off to disable it.

If these don't work, try unplugging the TV when you're done watching it each night (no, not ideal), using a smart plug to automatically cut the power for you or consulting Roku's tech support portal at support.roku.com for further assistance.

Liverpool transfer news: 'Significant' interest in £29m centre-back as Andoni Iraola ponders future without Virgil van Dijk

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

By Lewis Nolan | 14 Nov 2026 23:43

Liverpool are keen on Lens centre-back Pierre Ismaelo Ganiou despite interest from a number of other clubs, the latest report has claimed.

The Premier League is now on pause for the November international break, and that has given Reds supporters the chance to assess the squad.

Andoni Iraola has made a positive start to life at Anfield, with the team's defence having notably improved, led by the likes of Virgil van Dijk and Ronald Araujo.

However, Van Djik has entered the final year of his contract, while Araujo is only on loan from Barcelona and could return to the Spanish side at the end of the season.

Amid uncertainty regarding the future of their defence, Football Insider report that the Reds are one of several clubs keen on signing Lens defender Ganiou, who is valued by Transfermarkt at just over £25m.

Ganiou does not have an extensive history of top-flight football considering he has played just 28 games in Ligue 1 in his entire career, though he has been impressive for Lens.

In five league matches for the French side this season, the 21-year-old has won 69% of his duels, including 73% of his ground duels.

Liverpool have typically looked to sign aerially dominant defenders, so while the Frenchman boasts an impressive 64% duel success rate in the air, he only averages 1.8 aerial duels won per game.

Van Dijk has won an average of 3.8 duels in the air in the league so far, while Jeremy Jacquet has won 2.6 aerial duels in the Premier League, and the Reds would almost certainly aim to get Ganiou closer to those figures.

While Van Dijk is 35 and is into the final year of his contract, he has shown no signs of physical decline, and he has expressed a desire to remain at Anfield.

If he was to extend his stay on Merseyside, the Reds may not need to delve into the market for another centre-back.

Joe Gomez is expected to leave, and he could be replaced in the squad by Araujo, which would then leave the club in need of a right-back.

Jacquet and Giovanni Leoni will be part of the squad for the coming seasons, while many believe that Ifeanyi Ndukwe could play a role for the team once his loan at Levante ends.

Inside the First AI Music Awards, a Chaotic Evening Attempting to Normalize Tech-Assisted Tunes

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I knew things were a little off when the first thing I heard on my walk to the AI Music Awards was “the winner…” followed by the sweet voice of Kelly Clarkson.

No, Miss American Idol did not abandon her feud with the Brooklyn bird to capitalize on her newfound appreciation for AI. But as I approached the Mayan Theater in Downtown Los Angeles on a sunny afternoon to check in for Friday’s inaugural “AI Music Awards,” powered by the “Sonic Intelligence Academy” or “SIQA,” I wondered if her voice was so strong as to echo through the nearly century-old theater-turned-nightclub’s walls. Alas, not so: It was Clarkson’s Christmas classic “Underneath the Tree,” playing from speakers at a local YWCA event across the street. Related Stories The Best 'Real Housewives' Merch to Gift Bravoholics in 2026

To qualify for the awards, artists needed to register their tracks to SIQA’s “Verified Registry,” which denotes whether the song was fully AI-generated, made with the assistance of AI or featured a cloned human voice to lead the tune (what the group calls a “human + AI hybrid”).

Registered artists then nominate their peers in the 25 categories up for consideration — everything from “AI Song of the Year” to “Best AI Pop Song” to “Best AI Comedic Song” — and then vote on the winners. One of those nominees was Xania Monet, the AI persona of Telisha “Nikki” Jones, who a day earlier won the AI Album of the Year category at SIQA’s inaugural summit and who would eventually go on to win both artist and song of the year, three out of four of the awards’ top categories. (The fourth, producer of the year, went to “DJ Shawn Q.”)

The Sonic Intelligence Academy is the brainchild of software engineer Timirah “TJ” James and her sister Mia James. Since its January launch, it has worked to establish an awards program to counter the mainstream music industry’s aversion to AI. The group believes there needs to be infrastructure that supports those using AI to create music, rather than dismissing its existence, and it hopes to be an AI counterpart to the longstanding Recording Academy that governs the Grammy Awards. It advocates for artists to publicly associate themselves with their AI-powered work, eager to highlight the humans who helped create the product.

It’s a noble, if surprising, decision to embrace its role as counterculture when AI artists continue to pursue a Billboard charting position. Some of its methods are a little overt, perhaps intentionally: After some in the industry spurned the idea of AI-generated music charting on Billboard’s industry-standard charts, the group used a week in January ahead of the Grammys to launch its own charts exclusively for AI music. The Mayan is a 12-minute walk from the Crypto.com Arena, the Grammys’ longtime home.

The moves are not subtle, but subtlety is not the organization’s goal. The group and awards’ existence alone reflects those in the AI music space trying to move past the thorny legal questions surrounding AI music, which have led to lawsuits by artists and record labels against generators like Suno and Udio over copyright infringement, and into a realm of relevance within the genres they’re trying to embody.

It is, in a word, new, and new things mean kinks to work out.

The first kink I noticed was when, as I arrived 30 minutes before the red carpet was set to begin, the doors to the venue were locked. I had to pull on each door before someone opened one, which led me to the check-in stand (bypassing any form of security, which didn’t seem to exist). I entered the foyer and arrived at the makeshift red carpet, where about 12, mostly independent media ventures set up cameras to interview the various nominees, presenters, and VIPs who’d grace the roughly 7-foot-long red carpet in front of one of the two step-and-repeat backdrops. A spokesperson told me 29 media outlets had signed up to cover the event, but that some couldn’t make it because of the sudden flight ground-stop issued by the Federal Aviation Administration around 12:30 p.m. PT. Whether human or AI, aviation afflictions come for us all.

The guests gracing the red carpet were not screens rolled out displaying AI avatars, contrary to a reasonable assumption. Instead, the human creators behind the digital faces strutted the carpet in their Sunday best, treating the night less as music’s biggest night and more as the biggest night for their music. Sequined dresses and colorful blazers strolled through, stopping to speak with outlets like Reuters to the fiery red mohawk-sporting, sequin-wearing host of “The Iconic Punk Oz” Oz Powers to, tucked against a wall and steps from the red carpet, an effective AI red carpet correspondent housed inside a tall, white faux-stone vessel. The contraption was developed by the event’s top sponsor, Deepgram, an AI startup specializing in text-to-speech agents.

The device, which knew the artists and pressed them about the event, struck in me a slight fear about whether people might opt for robot reporters instead of humans, though that fear subsided when I realized this device could not appear on the red carpet because it could not move. The creators seemed more eager to speak to the physical reporters anyway, with some of them emphasizing the human elements of their AI work. Fredro Starr, one half of the rap group Onyx, spent much of the carpet touting the human vocals that made up his AI-beat-laden record “It All Started in Brooklyn,” which he claimed was the first AI album on vinyl.

“You can’t replace [humans],” Starr told me, using the carpet to debut his new moniker, “Mad AI.” “The feeling that you get was what we bring with the spirit of hip-hop. So vocally, you can hear that, but the AI just took it to another level.”

Inside the theater, SIQA tried to bring a party-like feel to the function. A bar at the back of the venue —at a charge — kept well drinks flowing, and the bar top was littered with copies of a sponsored version of the newspaper “Rolling Out” featuring interviews with many of the nominees and James, the SIQA founder. One of those featured, Xania Monet founder Jones, sparked a wave of controversy last year when some of her Monet songs hit some of Billboard’s genre charts, leading to an interview with CBS’s Gayle King and to Kehlani slamming a $3 million record deal Jones received. As she waited for a drink by the bar ahead of the event’s start, Jones told me she wasn’t worried about the AI of it all overshadowing the pursuit of legitimacy within the greater music industry.

“AI is just a tool,” she said. “It just emphasizes what we do. I just feel like we should all be one.”

But despite that lofty goal, the kinks kept rearing their heads.

The event started about 30 minutes past its 6:15 p.m. start time, skipping a planned opening video montage for a dance break performed and choreographed by actor Giovanni Wilson with eight dancers to a seemingly AI-produced song that described — in literal terms — how we were at the inaugural AI music awards. The delay forced the show to skip introducing the nominees for the first half of the awards and instead have presenters just state the winner, robbing some attendees of hearing their names called for what felt like one of their biggest achievements.

The event was hosted by Wilson and podcast host Lana Hout, one half of the podcasting duo the “AI Chicks,” with a three-member house band of real people playing guitar, bass, keys and drums, soundtracking much of the night. The two hosts shared the only two microphones apparently available throughout the night, with a teleprompter loaded with the Claude-generated run-of-show, but they often seemed lost about what category, presenter, performance or commercial break was next.

At one point, Hout and Wilson were confused over whether they would cut to commercial or bring out the two presenters for best AI country artist. They then brought out the two country award presenters, only for Hout to walk back out mid-introduction to interrupt and say that, actually, they would go to commercial break. The award was eventually presented 13 minutes later with a different presenter.

Oftentimes, the use of AI seemed to hover over some of the technical hiccups. Deepgram was meant to power an AI voice that introduced the night’s presenters and nominees, but it often didn’t work and, when it did, talked over many of the presenters. Deepgram also had a commercial that played throughout the evening, but it would sometimes play at random moments even when a commercial break wasn’t called. The Deepgram team sat on the couch to my left, and when I looked over to see their reaction to the madness, one member had her head in her hands.

Early in the night, Wilson tried to introduce a trailer for his latest acting gig, the Terrance Howard-led film “A Good Hand Wasted” that he said used AI to help finish the project, but none of Wilson’s cues seemed to get it to play. Once it finally did, the screen only played a portion of the trailer before Howard’s face even appeared.

“So, ugh,” he said, before raising his hand to summon the audience’s applause.

The teleprompter was not much help, often shifting rapidly as presenters were speaking. The chaos led Wilson, who spent much of the night trying to maintain some decorum throughout the frustration, to joke they were “dealing with a prompter that has Tourette’s.”

The commercials for the event featured Deepgram and a new social network called “Trinity Social,” with the latter’s AI-generated ad featuring a persona wearing a necklace of the Rockstar Games logo encouraging a stadium full of generated people to sign up for the app. A Rockstar spokesperson did not respond to an immediate request for comment on whether it authorized the logo, though if it did not, the ad would represent the latest in AI content lifting copyrighted material without permission, all at an event celebrating a use of AI that has rankled the music industry over similar concerns.

Some of the greatest energy came from the evening’s nearly 10 performances, all of which made the cut despite the event running nearly 40 minutes behind schedule the entire night.

There had been some speculation over how, exactly, songs produced with AI and presented by AI avatars would play on stage. It turns out, through the power of lip-syncing and acting. Some acts, like country artist Ava Bentley and R&B artist EJ Rogers, lip-synced their entire performance, while others relied on hired performers who did even that for them. For “Chaisen Hale,” a project that features a video of an AI-generated boy with AI-generated vocals, a human lookalike singer named John Victor, 14, was brought out to lip-sync to the track. Attendees — including one duo, for some reason, dressed as Frankenstein and his bride — were generally riveted.

Nearly all of the performances were backed by the live band and dancers, who were provided to the artists for free by SIQA. Many who produce music with AI say they did so because they could not afford the studio musicians and resources required to bring their tracks to life, which made the reliance on the house band feel more like an opportunity to live out their dreams without the cost often paid by traditional artists who hire such musicians to produce their work.

Some of the only actual live vocals came from a rapper named GodFellow and Grammy-winning singers Eric Bellinger and Candace Wakefield, the latter of whom made a point to note the “hiccups” throughout the night and that she was singing live as she performed a song by AI artist “Aria Blue.” The performances captured much of what remained in the crowd, which grew smaller and smaller as the night progressed.

James, the SIQA founder, acknowledged the “technical difficulties” ahead of presenting Jones with her awards, but she promised those would be resolved ahead of next year’s ceremony.

“Because there will be a next year because this is the new music industry,” she told the crowd around 9:45 p.m., just as it was hitting its hard end time. ”SIQA has created the infrastructure for that industry and for all the artists coming up in that industry.”

And for many of the people in the room, that’s all they wanted. One artist, Sheena Taylor, accepted her award for best AI gospel song for a track called “I Denounce You, Satan,” by acknowledging the intense traumas she endured that led to a religious discovery. Another artist named Ramona Höffel, a 56-year-old woman who spends her days helping disabled people, flew in from Switzerland to represent her AI persona “Vireya” on her first-ever red carpet. (When I addressed her by her given name, she snapped at me.)

“I have a lot of ideas and wishes, and for a long time I paint, then I write, and that’s not enough,” she said, visibly nervous to be the first person to walk the red carpet. “The music gives me room to all this bring outside of my brain.”

I thought about Höffel’s words as I left the venue just after 10 p.m., well past the event’s scheduled 9:30 end, and into the downtown rain. To my right was a line forming to get into the venue for “Club 2010s” night, now delayed due to the prolonged awards show. It’s unclear whether AI music will generate an enduring monoculture in the way the music from the 2010s has, particularly as the larger music industry has mostly ostracized it. But it’s left people like Höffel feeling stronger, which is all someone like 2011 chart-topper Kelly Clarkson could ask for.

→ Baca artikel asli

England's Euro 2028 mission starts NOW! Thomas Tuchel's men must beat Spain to prove they can compete with the best - or risk their 'DNA' cursing them yet again, writes OLIVER HOLT

📅 2026-11-14  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Published: 16:10 EST, 14 November 2026 | Updated: 19:04 EST, 14 November 2026

It is four months, almost to the day, since England shrank from the call of history and lost their World Cup semi-final to Argentina in Atlanta but Thomas Tuchel may not believe that the Nations League tie against Spain in the Metropolitano on Sunday evening represents his best shot at redemption for the way things played out that afternoon in America’s South.

That is largely because Tuchel does not appear to believe he has anything to redeem himself for. The manner of his side’s capitulation - and the substitutions he made - in the Mercedes-Benz Stadium last July enraged the nation, but Tuchel said the fault was in the damned DNA of the English, that we were underlings, and that our players found it constitutionally impossible to keep hold of the ball.

Whatever Tuchel thinks, the fact remains that the tie against Lamine Yamal, Fabian Ruiz, Pedri and Mikel Oyarzabal and the rest of the world champions at the home of Atletico Madrid offers Tuchel and his players the opportunity to encourage themselves and their fans that they are not destined to be also-rans wired to fall at the final hurdle for evermore.

Spain are formidable opposition. They are on a record-breaking run of 43 matches unbeaten and have won their last 12 in succession. They haven't drawn a game since their June 15 game against Cape Verde in the World Cup. Their last defeat was on March 22, 2024, when they lost 1-0 to Colombia in a friendly. Some ask whether they are the best international side the world has ever seen.

‘Spain seem unbeatable,’ Tuchel said, when he walked into the press room at the Metropolitano on Saturday night and heard hymns of praise being lavished upon the opponents he is about to face.

‘They have a very strong identity and a very strong mentality. We are trying to take it away from them. We are up for it. We are up for the fight. We are up for a spectacular match. We can see how far we can push the limits with the world champions in Madrid. The group is full of self-belief and rightly so.’

England must beat Spain to prove they can compete with the best - even if they have flaws

Thomas Tuchel must show he can find a way around the limitations presented by the 'DNA' he has complained about

This may be the toughest game that Tuchel’s England side play until the European Championships in 2028, where the final will be contested at Wembley, and they will play all their games at home. They need to go into that tournament with the belief that they can beat the best when the pressure is at its highest.

Beating Spain away in the final game of the group phase of the Nations League is not quite the same as a critical match at a major tournament but it is as stiff a challenge as Tuchel is going to face until then. Beating Croatia 7-0 in front of gangs of schoolkids in Rijeka last month was hailed as the best England performance of the Tuchel era. Which says it all, really.

It was a great performance in a relatively meaningless match. Now it is time for him to show that he can marshal an England side to succeed against the very best, not fall apart when the prize is in sight. His protestations of powerlessness in the aftermath of the Argentina defeat were not a good look for a coach who was hired with the idea that his coaching prowess and his game management could get England over the line.

Has he learned anything from the manner of the Argentina defeat? ‘This game against Spain will be proof of concept at the highest level,’ he said, ‘but we are up for it. I think we have taken the next step after the World Cup. The best thing is to trust our process and trust ourselves. We need to be clear about how we want to impose ourselves and I think that is all visible.’

Now it is time for him to show that he can find a way past the limitations of England’s footballing DNA, find a way to compete with Spain and France when it really matters, and find a way to end the culture of near misses that has come to be England’s lot in the last decade of appearances in semi-finals and finals.

England have played six matches since that day of crushing disappointment in Atlanta and scored 22 goals. But in the only one of those matches that really mattered, they lost 3-2 to Spain at Wembley in the opening tie of their Nations League campaign at the end of September, a game in which they were largely outclassed by Luis de la Fuente’s team.

Spain are what England have to aim for. Tuchel mentioned their example and their ability and their mindset straight after the semi-final loss to Argentina. ‘Ball possession plays a crucial role,’ he said at his post-match press conference in Atlanta. ‘It’s maybe not in our DNA like it is in our Spanish DNA or in our Argentinian-Brazilian DNA, to take the ball and control the game with the ball.’

This may well be their toughest game until the final of the 2028 European Championships, if they get that far

The England coach said more recently that England need to go back to trusting a traditional style that emphasises direct, high-intensity physicality rather than trying to copy Spanish patience in ball-retention. England, though, were taught a lesson in sophistication by the Spanish in September.

And for all the attention lavished on Yamal, Spain’s defence is the best in the world. Rodri, the shield for that defence and also the world’s best in the role, is not fit for this game but even without him, Spain’s back-line inspires an awful lot more confidence than England’s.

‘They were the best in the World Cup at the back,’ Declan Rice, England’s central midfielder, said on Saturday. ‘Defence wins you titles. Everyone talks about their attacking talent but you look at their central defenders, their full backs and the way their midfielders contribute to the defence, too.’

That defence will have to stop England’s resurgent front line, led by Ballon d’Or winner Harry Kane, who could be flanked by Anthony Gordon and Morgan Rogers and backed up by the supreme talent of Jude Bellingham just behind them.

Bellingham’s improving relationship with Tuchel, which cannot have been harmed by the inclusion of Bellingham’s brother, Jobe, in this squad, is another reason to hope that England still retain the capacity to improve after the World Cup. The elder Bellingham has become England’s most influential player.

England at their best would be one of the few teams capable of extending them. Rice said on Saturday that the game between the two sides was ‘massive’. England need to prove they can find a way to get closer to the world champions. To beat them, Rice said, would be a ‘massive statement’.

‘Playing against them is a great incentive to win. It will show where we are as a group. We are trying to attack teams full throttle and with players like Harry Kane, Jude Bellingham and Morgan Rogers in our attack, it feels as if it is going to take off one night and maybe tomorrow night will be the night. We can beat Spain. For sure, we can.’

Tuchel needs to prove England can win, not just be noble contenders. They need to prove that their DNA need not be a curse.
